{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},(2,1)}[x_{1},x_{2}-x_{\overline{1}}\,;t]&=&x_{1}^{3}&+&t\,x_{1}^{2}x_{2}&+&t^{2}\,x_{1}x_{2}^{2}&-&t\,x_{1}^{2}x_{\overline{1}}&-&\,t^{2}x_{1}x_{\overline{1}}x_{2}\,.\end{array}

For comparison, we compute the flagged symmetric function
{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},(2,1)}(X_{1},X_{2};t)
by applying
\xi^{-1}
to
{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},(2,1)}[x_{1},x_{2};t]
, and then compute
{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},(2,1)}[x_{1},x_{2}-x_{\overline{1}}\,;t]
by specializing. Using
the value
{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},(2,1)}[x_{1},x_{2};t]=x_{1}x_{2}^{2}+t\,x_{1}^{2}x_{2}+t^{2}\,x_{1}^{3}
from part (i), this gives

(202)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} {\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},(2,1)}(X_{1},X_{2};t)&=\xi^{-1}(x_{1}x_{2}^{2}+t\,x_{1}^{2}+t^{2}\,x_{1}^{3})=\xi^{-1}(t^{2}\,{\mathfrak{h}}_{1,2}+(t-t^{2})\,{\mathfrak{h}}_{2,1}+(1-t)\,{\mathfrak{h}}_{3,0})\\
&=t^{2}\,{\mathfrak{h}}_{1,2}(X_{1},X_{2})+(t-t^{2})\,{\mathfrak{h}}_{2,1}(X_{1},X_{2})+(1-t)\,{\mathfrak{h}}_{3,0}(X_{1},X_{2}).\end{aligned}

(203)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} {\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}[x_{1},\,&x_{2}-x_{\overline{1}};t]\\
&=t^{2}\,h_{1}[x_{1}]h_{2}[x_{1}+x_{2}-x_{\overline{1}}]+(t-t^{2})\,h_{2}[x_{1}]h_{1}[x_{1}+x_{2}-x_{\overline{1}}]+(1-t)\,h_{3}[x_{1}]\\
&=x_{1}^{3}+t\,x_{1}^{2}x_{2}+t^{2}\,x_{1}x_{2}^{2}-t\,x_{1}^{2}x_{\overline{1}}-t^{2}\,x_{1}x_{\overline{1}}x_{2}\,.\end{aligned}

Example 6.4.13
.

Since
{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}[X_{1},\dots,X_{l}]
is a flagged symmetric
function, Proposition
3.2.1
applies to its
flag bound specializations. This implies that applying
\pi_{k}
to
the combinatorial formula in
Corollary
6.4.10
has the effect of changing
the flag bound
k
to
k+1
in the extended tableaux
T^{\prime}
, if the flag
bound
k
is not repeated. For instance, for
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}=((2,1))
and

\sigma=(2,1)
with flag bounds
(b_{1},b_{2})=(1,3)
or
(2,3)
, as
in Example
6.4.7
, we have

(204)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}[x_{1},x_{2}+x_{3};t]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\FSST({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma,{\mathcal{A}}^{+}(1,3))}t^{\inv(T)}x^{T}=x_{1}^{2}x_{2}+x_{1}^{2}x_{3}

(205)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}[x_{1}+x_{2},x_{3};t]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\FSST({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma,{\mathcal{A}}^{+}(2,3))}t^{\inv(T)}x^{T}=x_{1}^{2}x_{2}+x_{1}^{2}x_{3}+x_{1}x_{2}^{2}+x_{1}x_{2}x_{3}+x_{2}^{2}x_{3},

which agrees with
\pi_{1}(x_{1}^{2}x_{2}+x_{1}^{2}x_{3})=x_{1}^{2}x_{2}+x_{1}^{2}x_{3}+x_{1}x_{2}^{2}+x_{1}x_{2}x_{3}+x_{2}^{2}x_{3}
.

Example 6.4.14
.

We consider cases of Corollary
6.4.10
for
which
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
consists of a single shape. Note that
\sigma=w_{0}
is
then the only compatible permutation.

(i) When
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}=(\lambda/\mu)
is an ordinary skew shape,

\FSST({\boldsymbol{\nu}},w_{0},{\mathcal{A}}^{b}_{+})
is the set of semistandard
tableaux of shape
\lambda/\mu
such that all entries in the
i
-th
row are bounded above by
b_{i}
and each such tableau has
\inv(T)=0
. Hence
{\mathcal{G}}_{(\lambda/\mu),w_{0}}[X_{1}^{b},\ldots,X_{l}^{b}]=\sum_{T\in\FSST({\boldsymbol{\nu}},w_{0},{\mathcal{A}}_{+}^{b})}x^{T}={\mathfrak{s}}_{\lambda/\mu}[X_{1}^{b},\ldots,X_{l}^{b}]
by
Corollary
6.4.10

and (
56
). By Remark
3.1.7
, choosing
b
so that
X_{i}^{b}
are large enough gives

(206)

{\mathcal{G}}_{(\lambda/\mu),w_{0}}(X_{1},\dots,X_{l})={\mathfrak{s}}_{\lambda/\mu}(X_{1},\dots,X_{l})\,.

(ii) When
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}=((m,m+1,\ldots,m+l-1)/\mu)
is a single ‘totally
ragged-right’ skew shape, where
\mu
is a partition with
\mu_{1}\leq m
, we can show that

(207)

{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},w_{0}}[x_{1},\dots,x_{l};t]={\mathcal{A}}_{\lambda}(x_{1},\dots,x_{l}),

where
\lambda=(m-\mu_{1},m+1-\mu_{2},\dots,m+l-1-\mu_{l})
, as
follows. Apply Corollary
6.4.10
with flag
bounds
b=(1,2,\dots,l)
. Consider
T\in\FSST({\boldsymbol{\nu}},w_{0},\mathcal{A}_{+}^{b})
and let
T_{1}
(resp.
T_{2}
) be its restriction to columns
\leq m
(resp.
>m
). Then
T_{2}
is the tableau whose entire
i
-th row
is filled with
i
’s. This forces
T_{1}
to have entries
\leq i
in
row
i
, but every semistandard tableau of shape
(m^{l})/\mu
filled
with
1,\dots,l
already has this property. Since

s_{(m^{l})/\mu}(x_{1},\dots,x_{l})=s_{(m-\mu_{l},\dots,m-\mu_{1})}(x_{1},\dots,x_{l})
, we can use (
196
)
and Lemma
2.11.3
to find

(208)

\sum_{T\in\FSST({\boldsymbol{\nu}},w_{0},{\mathcal{A}}_{+}^{b})}t^{\inv(T)}x^{T}=x_{2}x_{3}^{2}\cdots x_{l}^{l-1}s_{(m^{l})/\mu}(x_{1},\dots,x_{l})={\mathcal{A}}_{\lambda}(x_{1},\dots,x_{l})\,.

Theorem
6.4.9
also implies that symmetric
LLT polynomials can be expressed in terms of flagged LLT polynomials.

Corollary 6.4.15
.

(a) If
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
is a tuple of ordinary skew shapes, then

(209)

{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}(X,0,\ldots,0;\,t)={\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}(X;\,t),

where
{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}(X;\,t)
is a symmetric LLT polynomial independent of
\sigma
.

(b) If
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
is a tuple of ordinary skew shapes, then for any
flag bounds
b
such that
b_{i}\geq i
for all
i
, the Weyl
symmetrization of the specialization
{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}[X^{b}_{1},\ldots,X^{b}_{l};t]
is the symmetric LLT polynomial

(210)

{\boldsymbol{\pi}}\,{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}[X^{b}_{1},\ldots,X^{b}_{l};t]={\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}[x_{1}+\cdots+x_{n};t].

(c) If any component of
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
is strictly ragged-right, then
{\boldsymbol{\pi}}\,{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}[X^{b}_{1},\ldots,X^{b}_{l};t]=0
.

Proof.

If
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
is a tuple of ordinary skew diagrams, then every
box in
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
is
1
-admissible. A flagged tableaux
T
with all
entries in
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
is just a usual super tableau, and the
definition of
\inv(T)
reduces to the definition in §
2.8
for symmetric LLT polynomials, since pairs

(x,y)
in which
y
is a flag box do not contribute.
This given,
Theorem
6.4.9
proves (a).
Part (b) then follows from Corollary
3.2.4
.

If any component of
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
is strictly ragged-right,
then at least one box is not
1
-admissible, and
Theorem
6.4.9
implies that
{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}(X,0,\ldots,0;\,t)=0
.
Hence (c) follows using Corollary
3.2.4
again.
∎

6.5.
Proof of the combinatorial formula

We prove Theorem
6.4.9
at the end of this
section after some preliminary results, starting with lemmas that
relate attacking inversions (Definition
6.4.8
) to
triples (§
6.2
). The first two lemmas apply to fillings
more general than tableaux.

Definition 6.5.1
.

For a signed alphabet
{\mathcal{A}}
and a tuple of general diagrams

{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
, let
T\colon{\boldsymbol{\nu}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{A}}
be a
row-increasing super filling. A triple
(x,y,z)
of
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
is
increasing
if
T(x)\preccurlyeq T(y)\preccurlyeq T(z)
, in the notation of
(
23
), where if
x
and/or
z
is one of the extra
boxes in an extended row, we set
T(x)=-\infty
if
x\not\in{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
and
T(z)=\infty
if
z\not\in{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
. The number of
increasing triples in
T
is denoted
h(T)
.

Example 6.5.2
.

For flagged LLT indexing data
{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma
with
{\mathbf{r}}=(2,1,2)
,

\beta=(3,2,1,3,4)
,
\alpha=(1,0,1,1,1)
, and
\sigma=(2,3,5,4,1)
,
the picture on the left below shows the tuple of diagrams
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
,
with flag numbers written in the flag boxes. The picture on the right
shows the extended tableau
T^{\prime}
of a flagged tableau

T\in\FSST({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma,\mathcal{A})
, where
{\mathcal{A}}=\{1<\overline{1}<2<\overline{2}<\cdots\}
, with
{\mathcal{A}}^{+}=\{1,2,\ldots\}
,
{\mathcal{A}}^{-}=\{\overline{1},\overline{2},\ldots,\}
, and distinguished subsets
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}=\{1,\overline{1},2\},\,{\mathcal{A}}_{2}=\{\overline{2},3,\overline{3},4\},\,{\mathcal{A}}_{3}=\{\overline{4},5\},\,{\mathcal{A}}_{4}=\{6,7\},\,{\mathcal{A}}_{5}=\{8,\overline{8},9\}
. Flag bound
b_{i}
is written in the flag box
with flag number
i
, where we have chosen
b=(\overline{2},4,\overline{5},7,9)
.

{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
\vphantom{1}{}^{\#}\!\mathsf{1}
\vphantom{1}{}^{\#}\!\mathsf{5}
\vphantom{1}{}^{\#}\!\mathsf{4}
\vphantom{1}{}^{\#}\!\mathsf{2}
\vphantom{1}{}^{\#}\!\mathsf{3}
T
\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[6.02773pt][1.29167pt]{2}}
9
7
4
\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[6.02773pt][1.29167pt]{5}}
1
8
8
2
4
\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[6.02773pt][1.29167pt]{1}}
\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[6.02773pt][1.29167pt]{1}}
\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[6.02773pt][1.29167pt]{1}}
\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[6.02773pt][1.29167pt]{4}}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} h({\boldsymbol{\nu}})&=6&\qquad h(T)&=2&\qquad\inv_{{\rm o}}(T)&=4\\
e({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma)&=4&e(T)&=1&\inv(T)&=5\\
&&m(T)&=4&\inv_{{\rm o}}(T^{\prime})&=12\end{aligned}

For this choice of
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
,
\sigma
, and
T
, the values of the
following statistics are shown:
\inv(T)
counts attacking inversions
of
T
, as in Definition
6.4.8
,
\inv_{{\rm o}}(T)
counts
attacking inversions in
T
considered as a filling of
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}

without the flag boxes, as in Definition
2.8.1
,
m(T)

counts negative letters in
T
,
h({\boldsymbol{\nu}})
counts triples of

{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
, as in Definition
6.2.1
,
h(T)
counts increasing
triples in
T
,
e({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma)
counts rising end triples, as in
Definition
6.2.2
, and
e(T)
counts rising end triples

(x,y,z)
for
({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma)
which are increasing for the
extended tableau
T^{\prime}
—that is, in which either
x\not\in{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
,
or
T(x)\preccurlyeq T^{\prime}(y)
.

We will continue to use the notations
\inv(T)
,
\inv_{{\rm o}}(T)
,
m(T)
,

h({\boldsymbol{\nu}})
,
h(T)
,
e({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma)
, and
e(T)
for flagged
tableaux
T
throughout this subsection.

Lemma 6.5.3
.

Given
{\mathcal{A}}
,
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
, and
T
as in
Definition
6.5.1
, let
T^{-1}(a)
be the subdiagram
of
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
consisting of boxes
x
with
T(x)=a
, for each
a\in{\mathcal{A}}
. If
T
has no entries equal to
a
in a row
\beta_{r}/\alpha_{r}
of
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
, we consider the corresponding row of

T^{-1}(a)
to be an empty row
\gamma/\gamma
for the unique

\gamma\in[\alpha_{r},\beta_{r}]
such that
T(x)<a
for all
x

in
\gamma/\alpha_{r}
, and
T(x)>a
for all
x
in
\beta_{r}/\gamma
. Then the number of increasing triples in
T
is given
by

(211)

h(T)=\sum_{a\in{\mathcal{A}}}h(T^{-1}(a)),

where
h(T^{-1}(a))
is the number of triples of the diagram

T^{-1}(a)
.

Proof.

Consider a triple
(x,y,z)
of
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
, and let
a=T(y)
. Then
(x,y,z)
is a triple of
T^{-1}(a)
if and only if the
border between boxes
x
and
z
is weakly between the endpoints of a
row of
T^{-1}(a)
. With the convention that
T(x)=-\infty
if

x\not\in{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
and
T(z)=\infty
if
z\not\in{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
, this
happens if and only if
T(x)\preccurlyeq a\preccurlyeq T(y)
. Thus, the triples

(x,y,z)
of
T^{-1}(a)
are exactly the triples of
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
which
are increasing in
T
and have
T(y)=a
. Formula
(
211
) follows.
∎

The next lemma is a variant of
[
6
, eq. (132)]
.

Lemma 6.5.4
.

For
{\mathcal{A}}
,
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
, and
T
as in
Definition
6.5.1
, we have the identity

(212)

\inv_{{\rm o}}(T)=h({\boldsymbol{\nu}})-h(T).

Proof.

Let
(x,y,z)
be a triple of
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
. If
x\in{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
, then

(x,y)
is an attacking pair, and if
z\in{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
, then
(y,z)
is
an attacking pair. Since we set
T(x)=-\infty
if
x\not\in{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
, we have
T(x)\succ T(y)
if and only if
x\in{\boldsymbol{\nu}}

and
(x,y)
is an attacking inversion in
T
. Similarly,
T(y)\succ T(z)
if and only if
z\in{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
and
(y,z)
is an attacking
inversion. We cannot have
T(x)\succ T(y)\succ T(z)
because

T(x)\preccurlyeq T(z)
by assumption. Hence if
(x,y,z)
is increasing,
neither
(x,y)
nor
(y,z)
is an attacking inversion, and if

(x,y,z)
is not increasing then exactly one of them is an attacking
inversion.

Since every attacking pair is either
(x,y)
or
(y,z)
in a unique
triple
(x,y,z)
, it follows that the number of attacking inversions
is equal to the number of triples that are not increasing, giving
(
212
).
∎

Lemma 6.5.5
.

Given flagged LLT indexing data
{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma
, and subsets
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}<\cdots<{\mathcal{A}}_{l}
of a signed alphabet
{\mathcal{A}}
, as in
Theorem
6.4.9
, let
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{\prime}
be the
tuple of extended diagrams consisting of
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
together with the
flag box in each row. For every flagged tableau
T\in\FSST({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma,{\mathcal{A}})
, we have

(213)

h({\boldsymbol{\nu}})+e({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma)-\inv(T)=h(T)+e(T),

with notation as in Example
6.5.2
.

Proof.

Let
\inv_{1}(T)
be the number of attacking inversions
(x,y)
of

T
in which
y
is a flag box. By definition,
\inv(T)=\inv_{{\rm o}}(T)+\inv_{1}(T)
. Using Lemma
6.5.4
, this implies
that (
213
) is equivalent to
\inv_{1}(T)=e({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma)-e(T)
.

Let
(x,y)
be an attacking inversion with
x\in\nu^{(i)}
,
y\in\nu^{\prime}\,{}^{(j)}
, and
y
a flag box. By the definition of attacking
pair, we either have
c(y)=c(x)
and
i<j
, or
c(y)=c(x)+1
and

i>j
. Let
p
be the flag box in the row of
x
. Of course,
y

itself is the flag box in its own row. Let
T^{\prime}
be the extended
tableau in (
192
).

Since
(x,y)
is an attacking inversion and
T^{\prime}
is row-increasing, we
have
T^{\prime}(y)\prec T(x)\preccurlyeq T(p)
. This rules out the case
c(y)=c(x)
for the attacking pair
(x,y)
, since
c(p)>c(x)=c(y)
implies

T^{\prime}(p)\preccurlyeq T^{\prime}(y)
.

We are left with the case
c(y)=c(x)+1
and
i>j
. Let
z
be the
box to the right of
x
, with content
c(z)=c(y)
. If
z
is not a
flag box, then
c(p)>c(z)=c(y)
again implies
T^{\prime}(p)\preccurlyeq T^{\prime}(y)
,
contrary to hypothesis. Hence
z=p
is a flag box, and since
T^{\prime}(z)\succcurlyeq T(x)\succ T^{\prime}(y)
,
(x,y,z)
is a rising end triple, but not
an increasing triple in
T^{\prime}
. Conversely, if
(x,y,z)
is a rising
end triple which is not increasing in
T^{\prime}
, then
x\in{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
and

T(x)\succ T^{\prime}(y)
is an attacking inversion with
y
a flag box.
This shows that
\inv_{1}(T)=e({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma)-e(T)
, as
desired.
∎

The last ingredient needed for the proof of
Theorem
6.4.9
is the analogous result for
symmetric LLT polynomials, which equates the combinatorial formula in
Lemma
2.8.2
with an algebraic formula given
by
[
6
, Theorem 5.5.4]
. We briefly recall the latter
formula—see
[
6
, §5]
for more details.

Let
{\mathbf{r}}
be a strict composition of
l=|{\mathbf{r}}|
. It follows from the
definition of nonsymmetric Hall-Littlewood polynomials (§
5.2
) that

(214)

E^{-}_{{\mathbf{r}},\lambda}(z;t^{-1})\mathrel{\overset{\text{{\rm def}}}{=}}\sum_{w\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}}(-1)^{\ell(w)}E_{w(\lambda)}(z;t^{-1})

is antisymmetric for the Hecke algebra
{\mathcal{H}}({\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{r}}})
acting
on
{\mathbbm{k}}[z_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,z_{l}^{\pm 1}]
, i.e.,
T_{w}\,E^{-}_{{\mathbf{r}},\lambda}(z;\,t^{-1})=(-1)^{\ell(w)}\,E^{-}_{{\mathbf{r}},\lambda}(z;\,t^{-1})
for
w\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}
. Up to sign,
E^{-}_{{\mathbf{r}},\lambda}(z;\,t^{-1})
clearly depends only on the
{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}
orbit of

\lambda
, and vanishes if
\lambda
is not
\GL_{{\mathbf{r}}}
regular.
The set
\{E^{-}_{{\mathbf{r}},\lambda}(z;\,t^{-1})\mid\lambda\in X_{++}(\GL_{{\mathbf{r}}})\}
is a basis of the space of
{\mathcal{H}}({\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{r}}})
antisymmetric elements in
{\mathbbm{k}}[{\mathbf{z}}^{\pm 1}]
. These
elements are
E^{\operatorname{id},-}_{{\mathbf{r}},\lambda}(x;t^{-1})
in
the notation of
[
6
, eq. (116)]
. Using
[
6
, Remark
5.4.2(i)]
, we can formulate
[
6
, Theorem 5.5.4]

as follows.

Proposition 6.5.6
.

Let
{\mathbf{r}}
be a strict composition of
l=|{\mathbf{r}}|
. If
\gamma,\eta\in X_{++}(\GL_{{\mathbf{r}}})
satisfy
\gamma_{i}\leq\eta_{i}
for all

i
, then

(215)

t^{h({\boldsymbol{\nu}})}{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}(X;t^{-1})=\langle E^{-}_{{\mathbf{r}},\eta}(z;t^{-1})\rangle\,\Omega[X(z_{1}+\cdots+z_{l})]\,E^{-}_{{\mathbf{r}},\gamma}(z;t^{-1}),

where
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
is the diagram of
({\mathbf{r}},\gamma,\eta)
, as in
Definition
6.1.4
(since both
\gamma
and

\eta
are
\GL_{{\mathbf{r}}}
regular,
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
is a tuple of ordinary
skew diagrams).

If
\gamma,\eta\in X_{++}(\GL_{{\mathbf{r}}})
do not satisfy
\gamma_{i}\leq\eta_{i}
for all
i
, then the right hand side of
(
215
) is equal to zero.

Regarding formula (
215
), note that since
\Omega[X(z_{1}+\cdots+z_{l})]
is symmetric in
z
, multiplication by it
commutes with the Hecke algebra action and therefore preserves the
space of
{\mathcal{H}}({\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{r}}})
antisymmetric elements.

Corollary 6.5.7
.

For
\gamma\in X_{++}(\GL_{{\mathbf{r}}})
,
\eta\in X_{+}(\GL_{{\mathbf{r}}})
,
and
v\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}
, we have

(216)

\sum_{w\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}}(-1)^{\ell(w)}\langle F_{w(\gamma)}(z;\,t^{-1})\rangle\,\Omega[X\,\overline{z_{1}+\cdots+z_{l}}]\,F_{v(\eta)}(z;\,t^{-1})=(-1)^{\ell(v)}\,t^{-h({\boldsymbol{\nu}})}\,{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}(X;\,t)

if
\eta
is
\GL_{{\mathbf{r}}}
regular and
\gamma_{i}\leq\eta_{i}
for
all
i
, where
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
is diagram of
({\mathbf{r}},\gamma,\eta)
.
Otherwise, the left hand side of (
216
) is equal to
zero.

Proof.

Inverting the variables
z_{i}
and
t
turns the left hand side of
(
216
) into

(217)

\sum_{w\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}}(-1)^{\ell(w)}\langle\overline{F_{w(\gamma)}(z;\,t^{-1})}\rangle\,\Omega[X\,(z_{1}+\cdots+z_{l})]\,\overline{F_{v(\eta)}(z;\,t^{-1})}.

By Lemma
5.2.1
(a) and the definition of
E^{-}_{{\mathbf{r}},\lambda}(z;t^{-1})
, this is equal to

(218)

\displaystyle\langle E^{-}_{{\mathbf{r}},\gamma}(z;t^{-1}),\,\Omega[X\,(z_{1}

\displaystyle+\cdots+z_{l})]\,\overline{F_{v(\eta)}(z;\,t^{-1})}\rangle_{t}

\displaystyle=\langle\Omega[X\,(z_{1}+\cdots+z_{l})]\,E^{-}_{{\mathbf{r}},\gamma}(z;t^{-1}),\,\overline{F_{v(\eta)}(z;\,t^{-1})}\rangle_{t}

\displaystyle=\langle E_{v(\eta)}(z;t^{-1})\rangle\,\Omega[X\,(z_{1}+\cdots+z_{l})]\,E^{-}_{{\mathbf{r}},\gamma}(z;t^{-1}).

Now, for any
{\mathcal{H}}({\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{r}}})
antisymmetric element

f(z)\in{\mathbbm{k}}[{\mathbf{z}}^{\pm 1}]
, the coefficients of
f(z)
in terms of

E_{\mu}(z;t^{-1})
can be computed by first expanding in the

E^{-}_{{\mathbf{r}},\lambda}(z;t^{-1})
basis and then expanding each

E^{-}_{{\mathbf{r}},\lambda}
into
E_{\mu}
’s using (
214
). This
gives

(219)

\langle E_{v(\eta)}(z;t^{-1})\rangle\,f(z)=\begin{cases}(-1)^{\ell(v)}\,\langle E^{-}_{{\mathbf{r}},\eta}(z;t^{-1})\rangle\,f(z)&\text{if $\eta$ is $\GL_{{\mathbf{r}}}$ regular}\\
0&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

where
\eta\in X_{+}(\GL_{{\mathbf{r}}})
. Applying this with
f(z)=\Omega[X\,(z_{1}+\cdots+z_{l})]\,E^{-}_{{\mathbf{r}},\gamma}(z;t^{-1})

from the last line of (
218
), we see that the
corollary is equivalent to Proposition
6.5.6
.
∎

We now have the tools to establish our combinatorial formula for
flagged LLT polynomials.

Proof of Theorem
6.4.9
.

Given a strict composition
{\mathbf{r}}
of
l
, weights
\gamma\in X_{++}(\GL_{{\mathbf{r}}})
and
\eta\in X_{+}(\GL_{{\mathbf{r}}})
, and a
permutation
\sigma\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{l}
satisfying (iii), (iv) in
Definition
6.1.1
, define

(220)

G_{{\mathbf{r}},\gamma,\eta,\sigma}(x;t)=\sum_{w\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}}(-1)^{\ell(w)}\langle F_{w(\gamma)}(z;\,t^{-1})\rangle\,\overline{T_{\sigma}}\,\Omega[\sum_{i\leq j}X^{\mathcal{A}}_{i}/z_{l+1-j}]\,z^{\eta_{-}},

where
X^{\mathcal{A}}_{i}=\sum_{a\in{\mathcal{A}}_{i}^{+}}\,x_{a}-\sum_{b\in{\mathcal{A}}_{i}^{-}}\,x_{b}
. If we also have
\gamma_{i}\leq\eta_{i}
for
all
i
, then
{\mathbf{r}},\gamma,\eta,\sigma
are flagged LLT indexing
data, and the left hand side of (
194
) is
given by
{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}[X^{\mathcal{A}}_{1},\ldots,X^{\mathcal{A}}_{l};\,t]=t^{h({\boldsymbol{\nu}})+e({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma)}\,G_{{\mathbf{r}},\gamma,\eta,\sigma}(x;t)
, where
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
is the diagram of
({\mathbf{r}},\gamma,\eta)
.
By Lemma
6.5.5
, the right hand side of
(
194
) is equal to
t^{h({\boldsymbol{\nu}})+e({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma)}\,C_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}(x;t)
, where

(221)

C_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}(x;t)=\sum_{T\in\FSST({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma,{\mathcal{A}})}(-1)^{m(T)}\,t^{-h(T)-e(T)}\,x^{T}.

Thus, the theorem follows from the stronger assertion

(222)

G_{{\mathbf{r}},\gamma,\eta,\sigma}(x;t)=\begin{cases}C_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}(x;t),&\text{if $\gamma_{i}\leq\eta_{i}$ for
all $i$, where ${\boldsymbol{\nu}}$ is the diagram of $({\mathbf{r}},\gamma,\eta)$,}\\
0&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

which we will prove by induction on
l
. In the basis case
l=0
, the
condition
\gamma_{i}\leq\eta_{i}
holds vacuously,
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
is
the empty tuple,
\sigma
is the trivial permutation of
\emptyset
,
the unique element of
\FSST({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma,{\mathcal{A}})
is the empty
tableau, and (
222
) holds with
G_{{\mathbf{r}},\gamma,\eta,\sigma}(x;t)=1=C_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}(x;t)
.

We now derive a recurrence expressing
G_{{\mathbf{r}},\gamma,\eta,\sigma}(x;t)
for
|{\mathbf{r}}|=l>0
in terms of instances for
|{\mathbf{r}}|=l-1
. We will then prove (
222
) by verifying that the right
hand side satisfies the same recurrence.

Factoring out
\Omega[X^{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\,\overline{z_{1}+\cdots+z_{l}}]
from

\Omega[\sum_{i\leq j}X^{\mathcal{A}}_{i}/z_{l+1-j}]
and using the fact that
functions symmetric in
z
commute with the Hecke algebra action
yields

(223)

G_{{\mathbf{r}},\gamma,\eta,\sigma}(x;t)=\sum_{w\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}}(-1)^{\ell(w)}\langle F_{w(\gamma)}(z;\,t^{-1})\rangle\,\Omega[X^{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\,\overline{z_{1}+\cdots+z_{l}}]\,\overline{T_{\sigma}}\,\Omega[\sum_{2\leq i\leq j}X^{\mathcal{A}}_{i}/z_{l+1-j}]\,z^{\eta_{-}}.

By Corollary
6.5.7
, this is equal to

(224)

\sum_{\zeta}t^{-h({\boldsymbol{\lambda}})}\,{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}(X^{\mathcal{A}}_{1};\,t)\sum_{w\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}}(-1)^{\ell(w)}\langle F_{w(\zeta)}(z;t^{-1})\rangle\,\overline{T_{\sigma}}\,\Omega[\sum_{2\leq i\leq j}X^{\mathcal{A}}_{i}/z_{l+1-j}]\,z^{\eta_{-}},

where the outer sum is over
\zeta\in X_{++}(\GL_{{\mathbf{r}}})
satisfying

\gamma_{i}\leq\zeta_{i}
for all
i
, and
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
is the
diagram of
({\mathbf{r}},\gamma,\zeta)
.

Let
m=\sigma(l)
, and define
\widehat{\sigma}
by

(225)

\sigma=s_{m}\cdots s_{l-1}\,\widehat{\sigma}.

More explicitly,
\widehat{\sigma}
fixes
l
, and for
i<l
is given by

\widehat{\sigma}(i)=\sigma(i)
if
\sigma(i)<m
, or
\widehat{\sigma}(i)=\sigma(i)-1
if
\sigma(i)>m
. The factorization in
(
225
) is reduced, i.e.,
\ell(\sigma)=l-m+\ell(\widehat{\sigma})
, since
\sigma
has
l-m
inversions involving
l
, plus
inversions coming from
\widehat{\sigma}
. Therefore we also have

\overline{T_{\sigma}}=\overline{T_{m}}\cdots\overline{T_{l-1}}\,\overline{T_{\widehat{\sigma}}},
allowing the inner sum in
(
224
) to be rewritten as

(226)

\sum_{w\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}}(-1)^{\ell(w)}\langle F_{w(\zeta)}(z;t^{-1})\rangle\,\overline{T_{m}}\cdots\overline{T_{l-1}}\,\overline{T_{\widehat{\sigma}}}\,\Omega[\sum_{2\leq i\leq j}X^{\mathcal{A}}_{i}/z_{l+1-j}]\,z^{\eta_{-}}.

Since
\eta=\sigma(\eta_{-})
, we have
\eta_{m}=(\eta_{-})_{l}=\max_{i}(\eta_{i})
and
z^{\eta_{-}}\in(z_{1}\cdots z_{l})^{\eta_{m}}\,{\mathbbm{k}}[z_{1}^{-1},\ldots,z_{l-1}^{-1}]
. Since

\Omega[\sum_{2\leq i\leq j}X^{\mathcal{A}}_{i}/z_{l+1-j}]
is a power
series in the
x
variables with coefficients in
{\mathbbm{k}}[z_{1}^{-1},\ldots,z_{l-1}^{-1}]
, and
\overline{T_{\widehat{\sigma}}}\in{\mathcal{H}}({\mathfrak{S}}_{l-1})
preserves
{\mathbbm{k}}[z_{1}^{-1},\ldots,z_{l-1}^{-1}]
and commutes with multiplication by

z_{1}\cdots z_{l}
, it follows that
\overline{T_{\widehat{\sigma}}}\,\Omega[\sum_{2\leq i\leq j}X^{\mathcal{A}}_{i}/z_{l+1-j}]\,z^{\eta_{-}}

has coefficients in
(z_{1}\cdots z_{l})^{\eta_{m}}\,{\mathbbm{k}}[z_{1}^{-1},\ldots,z_{l-1}^{-1}]
. Using
Lemma
5.2.3
(a)–(b) and the fact that the

F_{\kappa}(z;t^{-1})
indexed by
\kappa\in-{\mathbb{N}}^{l-1}
form a basis
of
{\mathbbm{k}}[z_{1}^{-1},\ldots,z_{l-1}^{-1}]
, this implies that

\overline{T_{\widehat{\sigma}}}\,\Omega[\sum_{2\leq i\leq j}X^{\mathcal{A}}_{i}/z_{l+1-j}]\,z^{\eta_{-}}
can be expanded in terms of
F_{\mu}(z;t^{-1})
for
\mu
of the form
(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{l-1},\eta_{m})
with
\mu_{i}\leq\eta_{m}
for all
i
, and that the
coefficients in this expansion satisfy

(227)

\langle F_{\mu}(z;t^{-1})\rangle\,\overline{T_{\widehat{\sigma}}}\,\Omega[\sum_{2\leq i\leq j}X^{\mathcal{A}}_{i}/z_{l+1-j}]\,z^{\eta_{-}}=\langle F_{(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{l-1})}(z;t^{-1})\rangle\,\overline{T_{\widehat{\sigma}}}\,\Omega[\sum_{2\leq i\leq j}X^{\mathcal{A}}_{i}/z_{l+1-j}]\,z^{\widehat{\eta}_{-}},

where
\widehat{\eta}=(\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{m-1},\eta_{m+1},\ldots,\eta_{l})
, so
\eta_{-}=(\widehat{\eta}_{-};\eta_{m})
.

Note that the right hand side of (
227
) does not involve

z_{l}
, and that if we regard
\widehat{\sigma}
as an element of
{\mathfrak{S}}_{l-1}
by identifying
{\mathfrak{S}}_{l-1}
with the subgroup
{\mathfrak{S}}_{l-1}\cong\Stab_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{l}}(l)\subset{\mathfrak{S}}_{l}
, the operator

\overline{T_{\widehat{\sigma}}}\in{\mathcal{H}}({\mathfrak{S}}_{l-1})
acting on
{\mathbbm{k}}[z_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,z_{l-1}^{\pm 1}]
is just the restriction of the
original
\overline{T_{\widehat{\sigma}}}\in{\mathcal{H}}({\mathfrak{S}}_{l})
.

Compatibility condition (iv) in Definition
6.1.1

implies that
m
is the first element in its
{\mathbf{r}}
-block. Since

\zeta\in X_{++}(\GL_{{\mathbf{r}}})
is strictly decreasing on
{\mathbf{r}}
-blocks, if
\mu=s_{l-1}\cdots s_{m}\,w(\zeta)
is as in
(
227
), that is, if
w(\zeta)_{m}=\eta_{m}=\max_{i}(w(\zeta)_{i})
, then
w(m)=m
,
\zeta_{m}=\eta_{m}
, and

\zeta_{i}\leq\eta_{m}
for all
i
. Using
Lemma
5.2.3
(c), it follows that if
w
and
\zeta

satisfy these constraints, then

(228)

\langle F_{w(\zeta)}(z;t^{-1})\rangle\,\overline{T_{m}}\cdots\overline{T_{l-1}}\,\overline{T_{\widehat{\sigma}}}\,\Omega[\sum_{2\leq i\leq j}X^{\mathcal{A}}_{i}/z_{l+1-j}]\,z^{\eta_{-}}=\\
t^{-e_{m}(\zeta)}\,\langle F_{v(\widehat{\zeta})}(z;t^{-1})\rangle\,\overline{T_{\widehat{\sigma}}}\,\Omega[\sum_{2\leq i\leq j}X^{\mathcal{A}}_{i}/z_{l+1-j}]\,z^{\widehat{\eta}_{-}},

where
\widehat{\zeta}=(\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{m-1},\zeta_{m+1},\ldots,\zeta_{l})
,
v=s_{l-1}\cdots s_{m}\,w\,s_{m}\cdots s_{l-1}
considered as an element of
{\mathfrak{S}}_{l-1}\cong\Stab_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{l}}(l)\subset{\mathfrak{S}}_{l}
, and
e_{m}(\zeta)
is the
number of indices
i>m
such that
\zeta_{i}=\zeta_{m}
. If
w

and
\zeta
do not satisfy the specified constraints, then the left
hand side of (
228
) is equal to zero.

Let
\widehat{{\mathbf{r}}}
be the strict composition of
l-1
obtained from
{\mathbf{r}}

by changing
r_{k}
to
r_{k}-1
, or deleting it if
r_{k}=1
. Then
we have
\widehat{\zeta}\in X_{++}(\GL_{\widehat{{\mathbf{r}}}})
,
\widehat{\eta}\in X_{+}(\GL_{\widehat{{\mathbf{r}}}})
, and one can check that
\widehat{{\mathbf{r}}},\,\widehat{\zeta},\,\widehat{\eta}\,,\widehat{\sigma}
satisfy conditions (iii), (iv) in
Definition
6.1.1
. One can also check that if
w\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}
and
w(m)=m
, then
v\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathbf{r}}}}
, and

\ell(v)=\ell(w)
. We take the
l-1
alphabets
{\mathcal{A}}_{i}
in the
definition of
G_{\widehat{{\mathbf{r}}},\widehat{\zeta},\widehat{\eta},\widehat{\sigma}}(x;t)
to be

{\mathcal{A}}_{2},\ldots,{\mathcal{A}}_{l}
, so that

(229)

G_{\widehat{{\mathbf{r}}},\widehat{\zeta},\widehat{\eta},\widehat{\sigma}}(x;t)=\sum_{v\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathbf{r}}}}}(-1)^{\ell(v)}\langle F_{v(\widehat{\zeta})}(z;t^{-1})\rangle\,\overline{T_{\widehat{\sigma}}}\,\Omega[\sum_{2\leq i\leq j}X^{\mathcal{A}}_{i}/z_{l+1-j}]\,z^{\widehat{\eta}_{-}}.

Rewriting the sum over
w
in (
224
) as
(
226
), then substituting
(
228
) into this, and comparing the resulting
expression for
G_{{\mathbf{r}},\gamma,\eta,\sigma}(x;t)
with
(
229
), we obtain

(230)

G_{{\mathbf{r}},\gamma,\eta,\sigma}(x;t)=\sum_{\zeta}t^{-h({\boldsymbol{\lambda}})-e_{m}(\zeta)}\,{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}(X^{\mathcal{A}}_{1};\,t)\,G_{\widehat{{\mathbf{r}}},\widehat{\zeta},\widehat{\eta},\widehat{\sigma}}(x;t),

where the sum is over
\zeta\in X_{++}(\GL_{{\mathbf{r}}})
such that
\gamma_{i}\leq\zeta_{i}\leq\eta_{m}
for all
i
and
\zeta_{m}=\eta_{m}
, and
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
is the
diagram of
({\mathbf{r}},\gamma,\zeta)
, as before. Using the induction
hypothesis (
222
) for
G_{\widehat{{\mathbf{r}}},\widehat{\zeta},\widehat{\eta},\widehat{\sigma}}(x;t)
,
this becomes

(231)

G_{{\mathbf{r}},\gamma,\eta,\sigma}(x;t)=\sum_{\zeta}t^{-h({\boldsymbol{\lambda}})-e_{m}(\zeta)}\,{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}(X^{\mathcal{A}}_{1};\,t)\,C_{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}},\widehat{\sigma}}(x;t),

where the sum is now over
\zeta\in X_{++}(\GL_{{\mathbf{r}}})

satisfying the stronger condition that
\gamma_{i}\leq\zeta_{i}\leq\eta_{i}
for all
i
and
\zeta_{m}=\eta_{m}
,
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}
is
the diagram of
(\widehat{{\mathbf{r}}},\widehat{\zeta},\widehat{\eta})
, and for
C_{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}},\widehat{\sigma}}(x;t)
, we take the
l-1
distinguished subsets of
{\mathcal{A}}
in the definition of
\FSST(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}},\widehat{\sigma},{\mathcal{A}})
to be

{\mathcal{A}}_{2}<\cdots<{\mathcal{A}}_{l}
.

In particular, if we do not have
\gamma_{i}\leq\eta_{i}
for all

i
, then the sum in (
231
) is empty, and
G_{{\mathbf{r}},\gamma,\eta,\sigma}(x;t)=0
, as claimed. Assuming now that

\gamma_{i}\leq\eta_{i}
for all
i
, let
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
be the diagram
of
({\mathbf{r}},\gamma,\eta)
. What remains to prove is that, in the
notation of (
231
),

(232)

C_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}(x;t)=\sum_{\zeta}t^{-h({\boldsymbol{\lambda}})-e_{m}(\zeta)}\,{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}(X^{\mathcal{A}}_{1};\,t)\,C_{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}},\widehat{\sigma}}(x;t).

The conditions on
\zeta
in (
232
) are that the
diagram
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
of
({\mathbf{r}},\gamma,\zeta)
is a tuple of ordinary
skew diagrams contained in
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
, left-justified (row
i
has
left endpoint
\gamma_{i}
in both
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
), and
containing all of row
m
. The diagram
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}={\boldsymbol{\nu}}\setminus{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
of
({\mathbf{r}},\zeta,\eta)
is a tuple of ragged-right skew
diagrams in which row
m
is empty and is the first row in its
component. The diagram
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}
of
(\widehat{{\mathbf{r}}},\widehat{\zeta},\widehat{\eta})
is obtained by deleting the empty
m
-th row from its component

\mu^{(j)}
in
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
and shifting the surviving component

\widehat{\mu}^{(j)}
one unit to the left (compensating for the fact that
deleting the first row shifts it one unit down) so that corresponding
boxes have the same content in
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}
and in
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
.

Consider a flagged tableau
T\in\FSST({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma,{\mathcal{A}})
, and
set
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}=T^{-1}({\mathcal{A}}_{1})
. By Lemma
6.4.2
,
the
1
-admissible region
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}_{1}
is the largest tuple of
ordinary skew diagrams contained in
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
. Since entries in

{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
are smaller than other entries of
T
,
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
is a
tuple of ordinary skew diagrams left-justified in
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}_{1}
,
hence also in
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
. Since the flag number in row
m
is
1
,
boxes in this row are not
i
-admissible for
i>1
, hence
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}

contains all of row
m
. Letting
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}={\boldsymbol{\nu}}\setminus{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
, we have
T|_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\in\SSYT_{\pm}({\boldsymbol{\lambda}},{\mathcal{A}}_{1})

and
T|_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}\in\FSST({\boldsymbol{\mu}},\sigma,{\mathcal{A}})
, with

T|_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}
having no entries in
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
.

Constructing
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}
by deleting the empty
m
-th row of

{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
, as above, the flag numbers for
\widehat{\sigma}
in the rows of

\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}
are one less than the flag numbers for
\sigma
in the
corresponding rows of
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
. Let
T|_{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}}
be the
tableau obtained by transferring the entry of
T|_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}
in each
box of
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
into the corresponding box of
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}
. Then

T|_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}\in\FSST(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}},\widehat{\sigma},{\mathcal{A}})
, where the

l-1
distinguished subsets of
{\mathcal{A}}
are
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}<\cdots<{\mathcal{A}}_{l}
, as in the definition of
C_{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}},\widehat{\sigma}}(x;t)
.

Conversely, if
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
,
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
, and
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}
are the
diagrams of
({\mathbf{r}},\gamma,\zeta)
,
({\mathbf{r}},\zeta,\eta)
, and
(\widehat{{\mathbf{r}}},\widehat{\zeta},\widehat{\eta})
, respectively, as above, every
T_{1}\in\SSYT_{\pm}({\boldsymbol{\lambda}},{\mathcal{A}}_{1})
and
T_{2}\in\FSST(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}},\widehat{\sigma},{\mathcal{A}})
fit together to form a unique flagged tableau

T\in\FSST({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma,{\mathcal{A}})
with
T|_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}=T_{1}

and
T|_{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}}=T_{2}
. By Lemma
6.5.4

and the definitions of
{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}(X;t)

and
C_{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}},\widehat{\sigma}}(x;t)
, we have

(233)

\displaystyle t^{-h({\boldsymbol{\lambda}})}\,{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}(X^{\mathcal{A}}_{1};t)=\sum_{T_{1}\in\SSYT_{\pm}({\boldsymbol{\lambda}},{\mathcal{A}}_{1})}(-1)^{m(T_{1})}\,t^{-h(T_{1})}\,x^{T_{1}}

(234)

\displaystyle C_{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}},\widehat{\sigma}}(x;t)=\sum_{T_{2}\in\FSST(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}},\widehat{\sigma},{\mathcal{A}})}(-1)^{m(T_{2})}\,t^{-h(T_{2})-e(T_{2})}\,x^{T_{2}}.

It is clear that
m(T)=m(T_{1})+m(T_{2})
and
x^{T}=x^{T_{1}}\,x^{T_{2}}
, and Lemma
6.5.3
implies that

h(T)=h(T_{1})+h(T_{2})
. Triples
(x,y,z)
of
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}
in which

y
and
z
are flag boxes correspond bijectively to triples

(x^{\prime},y^{\prime},z^{\prime})
of
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
in which
y^{\prime}
and
z^{\prime}
are flag boxes not
in row
m
; specifically, the rows of
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
with flag boxes
y^{\prime}

and
z^{\prime}
correspond to the rows of
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}
with flag boxes
y

and
z
. Among these, the triples
(x,y,z)
counted by the quantity

e(T_{2})
in Lemma
6.5.5
correspond to the
triples
(x^{\prime},y^{\prime},z^{\prime})
counted by
e(T)
. The remaining triples

(x^{\prime},y^{\prime},z^{\prime})
counted by
e(T)
are those in which
y^{\prime}
is the flag box
with flag number
1
in row
m
,
z^{\prime}
is a flag box in a higher
component with content
c(z^{\prime})=c(y^{\prime})
, and either
x^{\prime}\not\in{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
, or
T(x^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
, i.e., row
i
of
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
is an empty
row, where
z^{\prime}
is the flag box in row
i
. These conditions are
equivalent to
i>m
and
\zeta_{i}=\zeta_{m}
. Hence,
e(T)=e(T_{2})+e_{m}(\zeta)
.

It follows that the term of
t^{-h({\boldsymbol{\lambda}})-e_{m}(\zeta)}\,{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}(X^{\mathcal{A}}_{1};t)\,C_{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}},\widehat{\sigma}}(x;t)
corresponding to
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
in
(
233
)–(
234
) is
(-1)^{m(T)}t^{-h(T)-e(T)}x^{T}
. Summing over all choices of
\zeta
,
T_{1}
,
and
T_{2}
gives (
232
).
∎

6.6.
Atom positivity conjectures

In Remark
6.4.11
, we saw that flagged LLT
polynomials are monomial positive; by
Corollary
6.4.15
, part (a), this property
generalizes the monomial positivity of symmetric LLT polynomials. We
now conjecture stronger
atom positivity
properties for flagged
LLT polynomials, generalizing Schur positivity of symmetric LLT
polynomials.

Given a Laurent polynomial
f(x)=f(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
with
coefficients in
{\mathbb{Z}}[t]
, we denote the coefficients in the expansion
of
f(x)
in terms of Demazure atoms by

(235)

\langle{\mathcal{A}}_{\lambda}(x)\rangle\,f(x)=a_{\lambda}(t),\quad\text{where}\quad f(x)=\sum_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{n}}a_{\lambda}(t){\mathcal{A}}_{\lambda}(x).

We say that
f(x)
is
atom positive
if
\langle{\mathcal{A}}_{\lambda}(x)\rangle\,f(x)\in{\mathbb{N}}[t]
for all
\lambda
.

We conjecture that
{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{l};t]

is atom positive in certain cases, and also that the coefficients of
certain atoms
{\mathcal{A}}_{\lambda}
are positive in all cases.

Conjecture 6.6.1
.

If
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
is a tuple of ordinary (non-ragged-right) skew diagrams
and
\sigma
is the standard compatible permutation, then

{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}[x_{1},\dots,x_{l};t]
is atom positive.

Conjecture 6.6.2
.

For any flagged LLT indexing data
{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma
, we have

(236)

\langle{\mathcal{A}}_{\lambda}(x)\rangle\,{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}[x_{1},\dots,x_{l};t]\in{\mathbb{N}}[t]

if the index
\lambda\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{l}
is either unimodal (weakly
increasing, then weakly decreasing) or anti-unimodal (weakly
decreasing, then weakly increasing).

Example 6.6.3
.

(i) For the tuple of skew diagrams
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}=((1),(2,2),(2)/(1))
and
standard compatible permutation
\sigma=(1,3,2,4)
, we have the
positive atom expansion

(237)

{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}[x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4};t]=t\,\mathcal{A}_{4200}+t\,\mathcal{A}_{4020}+t\,\mathcal{A}_{3300}+2\,t^{2}\mathcal{A}_{3210}\\
+t^{2}\mathcal{A}_{3201}+2\,t^{2}\mathcal{A}_{3120}+t\,\mathcal{A}_{3030}+t^{2}\mathcal{A}_{3021}+t^{2}\mathcal{A}_{2310}+t^{3}\mathcal{A}_{2220}+t^{3}\mathcal{A}_{2211}\\
+t^{2}\mathcal{A}_{2130}+t^{3}\mathcal{A}_{2121}+t^{2}\mathcal{A}_{1320}+t^{2}\mathcal{A}_{1230}+t^{3}\mathcal{A}_{1221}.

(ii) Atom positivity can fail if
\sigma
is non-standard, even if

{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
is a tuple of ordinary skew diagrams. For instance, for

{\boldsymbol{\nu}}=((1)/(1),(1)/(1),(1),(1))
and
\sigma=(3,4,1,2)
, we
have

(238)

{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}[x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4};t]={\mathcal{A}}_{2000}+t\,{\mathcal{A}}_{1100}+(t^{2}+t^{3}-t^{4}){\mathcal{A}}_{1010}\\
+t^{3}{\mathcal{A}}_{1001}+t^{2}{\mathcal{A}}_{0200}+t^{3}{\mathcal{A}}_{0110}+t^{4}{\mathcal{A}}_{0101}+t^{4}{\mathcal{A}}_{0020}+t^{5}{\mathcal{A}}_{0011}.

The only terms
{\mathcal{A}}_{\lambda}
in this example for which
\lambda
is neither unimodal nor anti-unimodal are
{\mathcal{A}}_{1010}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{0101}
. All the others have positive coefficient, illustrating
Conjecture
6.6.2
.

(iii) Atom positivity can fail if
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
is ragged-right, even if

\sigma
is the compatible permutation ‘closest’ to standard,
corresponding to flag numbers decreasing in the ordering of flag boxes
by (content, component number), but increasing by rows within each
component, as required. For instance, for
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}=((1,1)/(0,0),\,(0,1,2)/(0,0,0))
,
\sigma=(2,5,4,3,1)
, with end contents

(1,0,0,0,0)
and flag numbers
(1,5,2,3,4)
, we have

(239)

{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}[x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4},x_{5};t]=t^{4}{\mathcal{A}}_{10121}+t^{3}{\mathcal{A}}_{10130}-t^{3}{\mathcal{A}}_{20120}.

(iv) For
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}=((1),(1),(1))
, with standard
compatible permutation
\sigma=(1,2,3)
, we have

(240)

{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}[x_{1},x_{2},x_{3};t]={\mathcal{D}}_{300}+t^{3}\,{\mathcal{D}}_{111}+t^{2}\,{\mathcal{D}}_{120}+t^{2}\,{\mathcal{D}}_{201}+\left(t-t^{2}\right){\mathcal{D}}_{210},

showing that the conjectures cannot be strengthened to positive
expansion in terms of full Demazure characters.

Conjecture
6.6.1
also cannot be strengthened to

multi-headed Demazure positivity
, which means that the atom
coefficients are nonnegative and monotone decreasing in Bruhat order
on each
{\mathfrak{S}}_{l}
orbit of weights. A counterexample to this is

{\boldsymbol{\nu}}=((1),(1),(1),(1),(1),(1))
with standard compatible
permutation
\sigma=(1,2,3,4,5,6)
, for which we have

(241)

\langle{\mathcal{A}}_{212100}(x)\rangle\,{\mathcal{G}}_{\nu,\sigma}[x;t]-\langle\,{\mathcal{A}}_{212010}(x)\rangle\,{\mathcal{G}}_{\nu,\sigma}[x;t]=t^{8}+t^{9}-t^{10}\not\in{\mathbb{N}}[t].

By Proposition
3.2.6
,
Conjecture
6.6.1
extends to all flag bound
specializations.

Corollary 6.6.4
.

If Conjecture
6.6.1
holds, then for
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}

non-ragged-right and
\sigma
standard, the flag bound
specialization
{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}[X^{b}_{1},\ldots,X^{b}_{l};t]
is atom positive, for all choices of the flag bounds
b_{1}\leq\cdots\leq b_{l}
.

By Corollary
6.4.15
, part (a), the
symmetric LLT polynomial
{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}(X;t)
evaluated in
n
variables is equal to

{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}[X^{b}_{1},\ldots,X^{b}_{l};t]
for flag bounds
b_{1}=\cdots=b_{l}=n
. Since a symmetric
polynomial is Schur positive if and only if it is atom positive
(Remark
2.11.1
(i)),
Conjecture
6.6.1
and
Corollary
6.6.4
generalize the known Schur
positivity of symmetric LLT polynomials.
Conjecture
6.6.2
generalizes Schur positivity
of symmetric LLT polynomials in another way, as seen in
Proposition
6.6.10
, below.

A reformulation of the conjectures

Given flagged LLT indexing data
{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma
, let
({\mathbf{r}},\gamma,\eta)
be the weight data with diagram
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
, as in
Definition
6.3.1
. Specializing
X_{i}=x_{i}
in
(
182
) and using (
46
)
yields an identity

(242)

t^{-h({\boldsymbol{\nu}})-e({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma)}\,\langle{\mathcal{A}}_{\lambda}(x)\rangle\,{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{l};t]\\
=\sum_{w\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}}(-1)^{\ell(w)}\langle F_{w(\gamma)}(x;\,t^{-1})\rangle\,\overline{T_{\sigma}}\,\,x^{\eta_{-}}\,w_{0}{\mathcal{D}}_{-\lambda}(x)\,.

In particular, in the case when
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
is a tuple of
single-row shapes with equal end contents, and
\sigma
is the
standard compatible permutation, we have
{\mathbf{r}}=(1^{l})
,
\eta=(d^{l})
, and
\sigma=\operatorname{id}
. Hence, by
(
242
), Conjecture
6.6.1

reduces in this case to
\langle F_{\kappa}(x;\,t^{-1})\rangle\,w_{0}{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(x)\in{\mathbb{N}}[t]
for all
\mu
, that is, to the
conjecture that opposite Demazure characters are
F_{\kappa}

positive. In general, (
242
)
expresses the atom coefficients in
Conjectures
6.6.1
and

6.6.2
as signed sums of the coefficients

(243)

\langle F_{\kappa}(x;\,t^{-1})\rangle\,\overline{T_{\sigma}}\,\,x^{\eta_{-}}\,w_{0}{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(x)

in the expansion of an opposite Demazure character
w_{0}{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
, acted upon with an antidominant weight monomial
x^{\eta_{-}}

and a Hecke algebra operator
\overline{T_{\sigma}}
, in terms of
nonsymmetric Hall-Littlewood polynomials
F_{\kappa}
,
and yields the following reformulation of the conjectures.

Proposition 6.6.5
.

(a) Conjecture
6.6.1
is equivalent to the
positivity condition

(244)

\sum_{w\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}}(-1)^{\ell(w)}\langle F_{w(\gamma)}(x;\,t^{-1})\rangle\,\overline{T_{\sigma}}\,\,x^{\eta_{-}}\,w_{0}{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(x)\in{\mathbb{N}}[t]

holding for all
\mu
, and all
\gamma,\eta_{-},\sigma
satisfying (i)
\gamma\in X_{++}(\GL_{{\mathbf{r}}})
is

\GL_{{\mathbf{r}}}
-dominant and regular, (ii)
\eta_{-}\in-X_{+}(\GL_{l})
is
antidominant, (iii)
\sigma
is maximal in
{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}\,\sigma
,
and (iv)
\sigma
is minimal in
\sigma\Stab(\eta_{-})
.

(b) Conjecture
6.6.2
is equivalent to
(
244
) holding for all
\mu
unimodal or
anti-unimodal, and all
\gamma,\eta_{-},\sigma
satisfying (i)–(iii).

Proof.

By Definition
6.1.1
, if we set
\eta=\sigma(\eta_{-})
, then
({\mathbf{r}},\gamma,\eta,\sigma)
are flagged LLT indexing
data if and only
{\mathbf{r}}
,
\eta_{-}
,
\sigma
satisfy assumptions (i)–(iii)
and the extra condition
\gamma_{i}\leq\eta_{i}
for all
i
.
However, the second clause of (
222
), established in the proof
of Theorem
6.4.9
, shows that the
expression in (
244
) vanishes if we do not have

\gamma_{i}\leq\eta_{i}
for all
i
. Hence
(
244
) holds for
{\mathbf{r}}
,
\eta_{-}
,
\sigma

satisfying (i)–(iii) if and only if it holds when
({\mathbf{r}},\gamma,\eta,\sigma)
are flagged LLT indexing data. The additional assumption
(iv) is the definition of
\sigma
being the standard compatible
permutation.

This given, the equivalence of the conjectures with the specified
instances of (
244
) is immediate from
(
242
).
∎

Conjecture
6.6.1
can be reduced further, using
the following property of Demazure characters, which follows from a
theorem of Polo
[
45
]
(see also
[
41
]
,

[
42
, §2]
,
[
48
, Ch. 6]
).

Lemma 6.6.6
.

If
\eta_{+}
is a dominant weight, then the coefficients in the
expansion
x^{\eta_{+}}\,{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(x)=\sum_{\lambda}c_{\lambda}{\mathcal{D}}_{\lambda}(x)
are nonnegative.

Let
\Lambda_{i}=\varepsilon_{1}+\cdots+\varepsilon_{i}

(
i=1,\ldots,l-1
) be fundamental weights for
\GL_{l}
. Given

\sigma\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{l}
, set
\eta_{0}(\sigma)=-\sum_{i:\sigma_{i}>\sigma_{i+1}}\Lambda_{i}\in-X_{+}(\GL_{l})
.

Proposition 6.6.7
.

In Proposition
6.6.5
, part (a), it suffices to
take
\eta_{-}=\eta_{0}(\sigma)
.

Proof.

For any
\eta_{-}\in-X_{+}(\GL_{l})
, we have
\sigma
minimal in

\sigma\Stab(\eta_{-})
if and only if
(\eta_{-})_{i}<(\eta_{-})_{i+1}
for all
i
such that
\sigma_{i}>\sigma_{i+1}
. Hence
the set of such weights
\eta_{-}
is equal to
\eta_{0}(\sigma)-X_{+}(\GL_{l})
. For any such
\eta_{-}
, writing
\eta_{-}=\eta_{0}(\sigma)-\zeta
with
\zeta\in X_{+}(\GL_{l})
, we have

x^{\eta_{-}}\,w_{0}{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(x)=x^{\eta_{0}(\sigma)}w_{0}(x^{-w_{0}(\zeta)}{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu})
. By
Lemma
6.6.6
,
x^{-w_{0}(\zeta)}{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
is a
nonnegative combination of Demazure characters
{\mathcal{D}}_{\lambda}
.
Hence the positivity in (
244
) holds for a given

\eta_{-}
and
{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
if it holds with
\eta_{0}(\sigma)

and
{\mathcal{D}}_{\lambda}
in place of
\eta_{-}
and
{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
,
for each
{\mathcal{D}}_{\lambda}
that occurs in the expansion of

x^{-w_{0}(\zeta)}{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
.
∎

Note that we cannot use this argument to reduce part (b) of
Proposition
6.6.5
to
\eta_{-}
of a special
form, because the space spanned by Demazure characters
{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}

with unimodal or anti-unimodal index is not closed under
multiplication by dominant weight monomials.
Conjecture
6.6.2
does admit the following
simplification.

Proposition 6.6.8
.

The validity of Conjecture
6.6.2
in the
unimodal case is equivalent to its validity in the anti-unimodal case.

Proof.

The change of variables
x_{i}\mapsto x_{w_{0}(i)}^{-1}
sends
{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(x)
to
{\mathcal{D}}_{-w_{0}(\mu)}(x)
and
F_{\kappa}(x;t^{-1})

to
F_{-w_{0}(\kappa)}(x;t^{-1})
, commutes with the action of

w_{0}
on the variables, preserves antidominant weight monomials, and
changes
\overline{T_{\sigma}}
to
\overline{T_{w_{0}\sigma w_{0}}}
. Changing
{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}
to
w_{0}\,{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}\,w_{0}={\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{r}}^{\op}}
, we see that the change of variables

x_{i}\mapsto x_{w_{0}(i)}^{-1}
preserves the assumptions on
(
244
) in
Proposition
6.6.7
, part (b), while
exchanging unimodal weights
\mu
with anti-unimodal weights

-w_{0}(\mu)
.
∎

Remark 6.6.9
.

Computations suggest that unimodal and anti-unimodal weights
\mu

are not the only weights for which the positivity in
Proposition
6.6.7
, part (b) holds—or,
equivalently, for which the atom
{\mathcal{A}}_{\lambda}={\mathcal{A}}_{-\mu}

has
\langle{\mathcal{A}}_{\lambda}(x)\rangle\,{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{l};t]\in{\mathbb{N}}[t]
for every flagged LLT polynomial

{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}
. However, we do not have a specific
conjecture describing a larger class of weights with this property.

Special cases

To help motivate Conjecture
6.6.2
, we prove it
in the two extreme cases when the weight
\lambda
is dominant or
antidominant (thus, both unimodal and anti-unimodal).

Proposition 6.6.10
.

If
\lambda\in X_{+}(\GL_{l})
is dominant, then
\langle{\mathcal{A}}_{\lambda}(x)\rangle\,{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{l};t]\in{\mathbb{N}}[t]
for all flagged LLT data
{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma
of length
l
.

Proof.

As we saw in Remark
2.11.1
(ii), for any Laurent polynomial
f(x)
, the coefficient
\langle{\mathcal{A}}_{\lambda}(x)\rangle\,f(x)
for
\lambda
dominant is equal to the
coefficient
\langle\chi_{\lambda}(x)\rangle\,{\boldsymbol{\pi}}f(x)
of the
irreducible
\GL_{l}
character
\chi_{\lambda}
in the Weyl
symmetrization of
f(x)
. By Corollary
6.4.15
, parts (b) and (c),
the dominant weight case of Conjecture
6.6.2
is
therefore equivalent to the known Schur positivity of symmetric LLT
polynomials.
∎

Proposition 6.6.11
.

If
\lambda\in-X_{+}(\GL_{l})
is antidominant, then
\langle{\mathcal{A}}_{\lambda}(x)\rangle\,{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{l};t]\in{\mathbb{N}}[t]
for all flagged LLT data
{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma
of length
l
.

Proof.

The antidominant case of Conjecture
6.6.2
is
equivalent to the positivity in Proposition
6.6.5
,
part (b) holding for
\mu=-\lambda
dominant. But then
w_{0}{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}=x^{-w_{0}(\lambda)}
is an antidominant weight monomial, so
(
244
) in this case is the same as in the case with

\eta_{-}
replaced by
\eta_{-}-w_{0}(\lambda)
, and
\mu=(0^{l})
.

This reduces the result to the case
\lambda=(0^{l})
, which holds by
Proposition
6.6.10
.
∎

Computational evidence for the conjectures

By Proposition
6.6.7
,
Conjecture
6.6.1
follows from positivity in
(
244
) for all
\mu
when
\eta_{-}=\eta_{0}(\sigma)
,
\sigma
is maximal in
{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}\,\sigma
,
and
\gamma\in X_{++}(\GL_{{\mathbf{r}}})
. This can be checked for a given

\mu
by means of a finite computation: namely, for each
\sigma\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{l}
, expand
\overline{T_{\sigma}}\,\,x^{\eta_{0}(\sigma)}\,w_{0}{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(x)
in terms of the
F_{\kappa}(x;t^{-1})
,
and then evaluate the signed coefficient sums for all Young subgroups

{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}\subseteq{\mathfrak{S}}_{l}
such that
\sigma
is maximal
in
{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}\,\sigma
.

Using this method, we verified the reduction of
Conjecture
6.6.1
in
Proposition
6.6.7
by computer in the
following cases: (a) for all
\mu\in{\mathbb{N}}^{l}
satisfying
|\mu|\leq 8
for
l=4,5
; and (b) for randomly chosen
\mu\in[0,m]^{l}
in
several hundred examples with
m=8
and
l=5,6
, several dozen with

m=6
and
l=7,8
, and several with
m=8
and
l=8
.

Conjecture
6.6.2
takes more work to check,
since we must allow both
\mu
and
\eta_{-}
to vary. By
Proposition
6.6.8
, the anti-unimodal
case suffices. We verified the positivity in (
244
)
for anti-unimodal
\mu
and varying
\eta_{-}
in hundreds of cases
for
l=6,7
, and a few with
l=8
.

6.7.
Signed flagged LLT polynomials

For signed alphabets of a specific form, the specializations of
flagged LLT polynomials in Theorem
6.4.9

interact well with the nonsymmetric plethysm
\Pi_{t,x}
and the
Hecke algebra action, and will be needed for the study of nonsymmetric
Macdonald polynomials in §
7
. We now define these
specializations and develop their properties.

Definition 6.7.1
.

Given flagged LLT indexing data
{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma
of length
l
, the

signed flagged LLT polynomial with flag bounds

b=(b_{1}\leq\cdots\leq b_{l})
is the specialization

(245)

{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}[X_{1}^{b}-t\,\hbox to13.56pt{\vbox to9.02pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.77777pt\lower-4.50891pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.06944pt}{-3.00891pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}^{b};\,t^{-1}],

where
X^{b}_{i}=x_{b_{i-1}+1}+\cdots+x_{b_{i}}
, as in
Definition
3.1.2
, and we set
X^{\prime}=(0,x_{1},x_{2},\ldots)
, so
{X^{\prime}_{i\,}}^{b}=x_{b_{i-1}}+\cdots+x_{b_{i}-1}
. In the special case
b_{i}=i
, we use the notation

(246)

{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}^{-}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{l};\,t^{-1})\mathrel{\overset{\text{{\rm def}}}{=}}{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}[x_{1},\,x_{2}-t\,x_{1},\ldots,x_{l}-t\,x_{l-1};\,t^{-1}].

Note that we define signed flagged LLT polynomials to be
specializations of flagged LLT polynomials with parameter
t^{-1}
in
place of
t
. This turns out to be the correct definition to use in
§
7
, and is also needed for results below.

Our next result is a signed analog of
Corollary
6.4.10
.

Given flag bounds
b_{1}\leq\cdots\leq b_{l}\leq n
, we write
{\mathcal{A}}_{\pm}^{b}
for the signed alphabet
{\mathcal{A}}=\{1<\overline{1}<\cdots<n<\overline{n}\}

with
{\mathcal{A}}^{+}=\{1,\ldots,n\}
,
{\mathcal{A}}^{-}=\{\overline{1},\ldots,\overline{n}\}
, and the distinguished subsets

(247)

{\mathcal{A}}_{1}=\{1,\overline{1},2,\dots,b_{1}\},\,{\mathcal{A}}_{2}=\{\overline{b_{1}},b_{1}+1,\dots,b_{2}\},\,\ldots,\,{\mathcal{A}}_{l}=\{\overline{b_{l-1}},b_{l-1}+1,\dots,b_{l}\}\,.

The set of flagged tableaux on these alphabets is then denoted
\FSST({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma,{\mathcal{A}}_{\pm}^{b})
.
Explicitly, by Lemma
6.4.5
, a tableau
T\in\FSST({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma,{\mathcal{A}}_{\pm}^{b})
is a filling
T\colon{\boldsymbol{\nu}}\rightarrow\{1,\overline{1},\ldots,n,\overline{n}\}
such that
the extended filling
T^{\prime}
with fixed entry
b_{i}
in the flag box
with flag number
i
is a ragged-right super tableau.

Proposition 6.7.2
.

Given
b_{1}\leq\cdots\leq b_{l}\leq n
, the signed flagged LLT
polynomial with flag bounds
b_{i}
is given by

(248)

{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}[X_{1}^{b}-t\,\hbox to13.56pt{\vbox to9.02pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.77777pt\lower-4.50891pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.77777pt}{-3.00891pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}^{b},\,\ldots,X_{l}^{b}-t\,\hbox to13.81pt{\vbox to9.02pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.90276pt\lower-4.50891pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.90276pt}{-3.00891pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}^{b};\,t^{-1}]=\sum_{T\in\FSST({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma,{\mathcal{A}}^{b}_{\pm})}(-t)^{m(T)}t^{-\inv(T)}x^{|T|},

where
x^{|T|}=\prod_{u\in{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}x_{|T(u)|}
, with

|\overline{a}|=|a|\mathrel{\overset{\text{{\rm def}}}{=}}a
, and
m(T)
is the number of negative
entries.

Proof.

For
{\mathcal{A}}={\mathcal{A}}^{b}_{\pm}
, the plethystic alphabet
X^{{\mathcal{A}}}_{i}
in Theorem
6.4.9
becomes

X^{b}_{i}-t\,\hbox to13.56pt{\vbox to9.02pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.77777pt\lower-4.50891pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{

upon substituting
t\,x_{a}
for

x_{\overline{a}}
. Hence, (
248
) follows by
substituting
t^{-1}
for
t
in (
194
)
and then setting
x_{\overline{a}}=t\,x_{a}
.
∎

The next result is immediate from
Propositions
4.1.5
and

6.3.2
.

Proposition 6.7.3
.

For strictly increasing flag bounds
b_{1}<\cdots<b_{l}
, the
signed and unsigned specializations are related by nonsymmetric plethysm:

(249)

\Pi_{t,x}\,{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}[X_{1}^{b}-t\,\hbox to13.56pt{\vbox to9.02pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.77777pt\lower-4.50891pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.77777pt}{-3.00891pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}^{b},\,\ldots,X_{l}^{b}-t\,\hbox to13.81pt{\vbox to9.02pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.90276pt\lower-4.50891pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}^{b};\,t^{-1}]={\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}[X_{1}^{b},\ldots,X_{l}^{b};\,t^{-1}].

In particular, when
b_{i}=i
for all
i
, we have

(250)

\Pi_{t,x}\,{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}^{-}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{l};\,t^{-1})={\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{l};\,t^{-1}].

Example 6.7.4
.

Propositions
6.7.2
and

6.7.3
give a combinatorial formula for the
nonsymmetric Hall-Littlewood polynomials
E_{\mu}
in terms of signed
flagged tableaux on a tuple of rows. Specifically, combining
(
187
) and (
250
), we have

(251)

E_{\mu}(x;t^{-1})=t^{h({\boldsymbol{\nu}})}{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\operatorname{id}}^{-}(x_{1},\dots,x_{l};t^{-1})\\
=t^{h({\boldsymbol{\nu}})}\sum_{T\in\FSST({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\operatorname{id},{\mathcal{A}}^{b}_{\pm})}(-t)^{m(T)}t^{-\inv(T)}x^{|T|},

where
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
is the tuple of rows
((0)/(-\mu_{l}),\dots,(0)/(-\mu_{1}))
and the flag bounds
b
for
{\mathcal{A}}^{b}_{\pm}
are

(1,2,\dots,l)
. For example, for
\mu=(2,1)
, illustrating
\FSST({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\operatorname{id},{\mathcal{A}}^{b}_{\pm})
with the same
conventions as in Example
6.4.12
), we have

E_{21}(x;t^{-1})=&\!\!\!\!\!\!t\,(\,x_{1}^{3}&+\;t^{-1}x_{1}^{2}x_{2}&+\;(-t)t^{-1}x_{1}^{3}\,)&=x_{1}^{2}x_{2}\end{array}

Example 6.7.5
.

Continuing Example
6.4.12
, we confirm
(
250
) for
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}=((2)/(1),(2))

and
\sigma=(2,1)
. Specializing
x_{\overline{1}}=t\,x_{1}
in
(
203
) gives

(253)

{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}^{-}(x_{1},x_{2};t^{-1})=x_{1}^{3}+t^{-1}x_{1}^{2}x_{2}+t^{-2}x_{1}x_{2}^{2}-t^{-1}x_{1}^{2}(t\,x_{1})-t^{-2}x_{1}(t\,x_{1})x_{2}=t^{-2}x_{1}x_{2}^{2}.

Hence, by (
81
),
\Pi_{t,x}\,{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}^{-}(x_{1},x_{2};t^{-1})=x_{1}^{3}+t^{-1}x_{1}^{2}x_{2}+t^{-2}x_{1}x_{2}^{2}
, which agrees with our computation of

{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}[x_{1},x_{2};t^{-1}]
in
(
200
).

Another property of the polynomials
{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}^{-}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{l};\,t^{-1})
is that Hecke algebra operators
have the effect of exchanging flag bounds whenever it makes sense to
do so.

Proposition 6.7.6
.

(a) Given flagged LLT indexing data
({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma)
, assume that
the flag boxes with flag numbers
i
and
i+1
have the same content
and are in different components
\nu^{(j)}
and
\nu^{(k)}
of

{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
, so that
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma s_{l-i}
is also compatible
with
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
, and
({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma^{\prime})
differs from
({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma)
by exchanging flag numbers
i
and
i+1
. Then

(254)

{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma^{\prime}}^{-}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{l};\,t^{-1})=\begin{cases}t\,\overline{T_{i}}\,{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}^{-}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{l};\,t^{-1})&\text{if $j<k$},\\[4.30554pt]
t^{-1}\,T_{i}\,{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}^{-}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{l};\,t^{-1})&\text{if $j>k$}.\end{cases}

(b) If the flag boxes with flag numbers
i
and
i+1
have the same
content and are in the same (strictly ragged-right) component of

{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
, then

(255)

T_{i}\,{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}^{-}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{l};\,t^{-1})=-{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}^{-}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{l};\,t^{-1}).

Proof.

By Corollary
6.3.4
and
Proposition
6.7.3
, we have

(256)

t^{h({\boldsymbol{\nu}})+e({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma)}{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}^{-}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{l};\,t^{-1})=x^{\eta_{+}}\,T_{w_{0}\,\sigma^{-1}\,w_{0}}\,w_{0}\sum_{w\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}}(-1)^{\ell(w)}\,\overline{E_{w(\gamma)}(x;\,t)},

where
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
is the diagram of
({\mathbf{r}},\gamma,\eta)
. Flag boxes
numbered
i
and
i+1
having the same content means that
(\eta_{+})_{i}=(\eta_{+})_{i+1}
, so
T_{i}
commutes with
x^{\eta_{+}}
.

For part (a), either case in (
254
) implies the other,
since
t^{-1}\,T_{i}=(t\,\overline{T_{i}})^{-1}
, so we can assume
that
j<k
. Then
\sigma^{\prime}<\sigma
, so
w_{0}\,\sigma^{-1}\,w_{0}
has a reduced factorization
s_{i}\cdot(w_{0}\,(\sigma^{\prime})^{-1}\,w_{0})
, which implies
T_{w_{0}\,(\sigma^{\prime})^{-1}\,w_{0}}=\overline{T_{i}}\,T_{w_{0}\,\sigma^{-1}\,w_{0}}
. We also have

e({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma)=e({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma^{\prime})+1
, with flag numbers

i
,
i+1
contributing one more rising end triple for
({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma)
than for
({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma^{\prime})
. The
j<k
case of
(
254
) now follows from (
256
).

For part (b), flag numbers
i
and
i+1
must be in consecutive rows,

j
and
j+1
, say. Then
w_{0}\,\sigma\,w_{0}(i)=l-j+1
and

w_{0}\,\sigma\,w_{0}(i+1)=l-j
, which implies that
v\cdot s_{l-j}

and
s_{i}\cdot v
are reduced factorizations of
w_{0}\,\sigma^{-1}\,w_{0}
, where
v=(w_{0}\,\sigma^{-1}\,w_{0})\,s_{l-j}=s_{i}\,(w_{0}\,\sigma^{-1}\,w_{0})
. Hence
T_{w_{0}\,\sigma^{-1}\,w_{0}}=T_{v}\,T_{l-j}=T_{i}\,T_{v}
, and therefore

T_{i}\,T_{w_{0}\,\sigma^{-1}\,w_{0}}=T_{w_{0}\,\sigma^{-1}\,w_{0}}\,T_{l-j}
. As in (
214
), the sum

(257)

w_{0}\sum_{w\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}}(-1)^{\ell(w)}\,\overline{E_{w(\gamma)}(x;\,t)}=\sum_{w\in w_{0}\,{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}\,w_{0}}(-1)^{\ell(w)}\,E_{-w\,w_{0}(\gamma)}(x;\,t^{-1})

is antisymmetric for the Hecke algebra of
w_{0}\,{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}\,w_{0}={\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{r}}^{\op}}
, where
{\mathbf{r}}^{\op}
is the reverse of

{\mathbf{r}}
. Since
j
and
j+1
are in the same
{\mathbf{r}}
block,
l-j
and

l-j+1
are in the same
{\mathbf{r}}^{\op}
block. Hence the sum in
(
257
) is antisymmetric for
T_{l-j}
. This given,
(
256
) implies (
255
).
∎

Example 6.7.7
.

Consider
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}=((2)/(1),(2)/(0))
and set
\sigma^{\prime}=(2,1)
,

\sigma=\operatorname{id}
, so that
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma s_{1}
. We
have
{\mathcal{G}}^{-}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma^{\prime}}(x_{1},x_{2};t^{-1})=t^{-2}x_{1}x_{2}^{2}

from Example
6.7.5
, and a similar computation gives

{\mathcal{G}}^{-}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma^{\prime}}=t^{-1}x_{1}^{2}x_{2}
. One checks that
t^{-1}x_{1}^{2}x_{2}=t\,\overline{T_{1}}(t^{-2}x_{1}x_{2}^{2})
, confirming
the assertion of Proposition
6.7.6
that

{\mathcal{G}}^{-}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma^{\prime}}(x_{1},x_{2};t^{-1})=t\,\overline{T_{1}}\,{\mathcal{G}}^{-}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}(x_{1},x_{2};t^{-1})
in this
case.

A feature of the combinatorial formula in
Proposition
6.7.2
is the existence of a
sign-reversing involution that permits the sum in
(
248
) to be restricted to tableaux
T

satisfying a non-attacking condition.

Definition 6.7.8
.

With notation as in Proposition
6.7.2
, a
flagged tableau
T\in\FSST({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma,{\mathcal{A}}^{b}_{\pm})
is

non-attacking
if there is no attacking pair
(x,y)
for
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
such that
|T^{\prime}(x)|=|T^{\prime}(y)|
, where
T^{\prime}
is the extended tableau
with fixed entry
b_{i}
in the flag box with flag number
i
, and
|\overline{a}|=|a|\mathrel{\overset{\text{{\rm def}}}{=}}a
.

Proposition 6.7.9
.

With notation as in Proposition
6.7.2
, if the
flag bounds
b_{1}<\cdots<b_{l}
are strictly increasing, then formula
(
248
) also holds with the sum restricted to
non-attacking tableaux:

(258)

{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}[X^{b}_{1}-t\,\hbox to13.56pt{\vbox to9.02pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.77777pt\lower-4.50891pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.77777pt}{-3.00891pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}^{b},\,\ldots,X^{b}_{l}-t\,\hbox to13.81pt{\vbox to9.02pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.90276pt\lower-4.50891pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.90276pt}{-3.00891pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}^{b};\,t^{-1}]=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}T\in\FSST({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma,{\mathcal{A}}^{b}_{\pm})\\
\text{{\rm non-attacking}}\end{subarray}}(-t)^{m(T)}t^{-\inv(T)}x^{|T|}.

In particular, this holds for
{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}^{-}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{l};\,t^{-1})
.

Proof.

Adapting the proof of
[
20
, Lemma 5.1]
to flagged
tableaux, we will construct a sign-reversing, weight-preserving
involution
\Psi
on tableaux
T\in\FSST({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma,{\mathcal{A}})

that violate the non-attacking condition; then the terms in
(
248
) for
T
and
\Psi(T)
cancel, leaving
only the terms in (
258
).

The definition of attacking pair
(x,y)
is equivalent to:
x
and
y

belong to different components of
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{\prime}
,
x
is not a flag box,
and
x<y<z
in the content/row reading order, where
z
is the box
immediately to the right of
x
.

Given
T\in\FSST({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma,{\mathcal{A}}^{b}_{\pm})
which fails to
be non-attacking, let
x
be the last box in reading order that occurs
in an attacking pair
(x,y)
with
|T^{\prime}(x)|=|T^{\prime}(y)|
, and let
a=|T^{\prime}(x)|
. We claim that for this
x
, the other member
y
of the
pair is unique.

Suppose to the contrary that
(x,y)
and
(x,y^{\prime})
are attacking pairs
with
|T^{\prime}(x)|=|T^{\prime}(y)|=|T^{\prime}(y^{\prime})|=a
and (without loss of
generality) that
y<y^{\prime}
in reading order. If
y
is a flag box with
flag number
j
, then the flag number
i
in the row of
y^{\prime}
is less
than
j
, because the flag box in the row of
y^{\prime}
follows
y
in
reading order. But then
|T^{\prime}(y^{\prime})|\leq b_{i}<b_{j}=a
. Hence
y
is
not a flag box. For use below, note that this argument shows that if

|T^{\prime}(u)|=a
for any
u
following
y
in reading order, then
y
is
not a flag box.

If
y
and
y^{\prime}
are in the same component of
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{\prime}
, then they
have the same content, either
c(y)=c(y^{\prime})=c(x)
or
c(y)=c(y^{\prime})=c(x)+1
, depending on whether the component containing
y
and
y^{\prime}
is
above or below the component containing
x
. But then we cannot have

|T^{\prime}(y)|=|T^{\prime}(y^{\prime})|=a
, because
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
contains a box
v
above

y
and to the left of
y^{\prime}
, the condition on rows in a flagged
tableau implies that
T(v)\leq a
, and the condition on columns
implies that
T(v)\geq\overline{a}
. Hence
y
and
y^{\prime}
are in
different components.

Since
x<y<y^{\prime}\leq z
in reading order, where
z
is the box
immediately to the right of
x
, and
y
is not a flag box, and
y

and
y^{\prime}
are in different components, it follows that
(y,y^{\prime})
is an
attacking pair. But
y
follows
x
in reading order, so this
contradicts the choice of
x
. This shows that
y
is unique, as
claimed.

We construct
S=\Psi(T)
by changing the sign of
T(x)
, that is,

S(x)=a
if
T(x)=\overline{a}
, or
S(x)=\overline{a}
if
T(x)=a
, and
S(u)=T(u)
for
u\not=x
. We claim that this defines a
valid flagged tableau
S
. If not, then
S(u)
and
S(x)
would have
to violate the row or column conditions for a box
u
adjacent to
x

in its row or column. We now check and rule this out for each of the
four possible boxes
u
.

If
u
is the box to the left of
x
or above
x
, let
S(u)=T(u)=b
. If
u
is the box to the left of
x
, then
S(u)\preccurlyeq S(x)

because
b\preccurlyeq a\Leftrightarrow b\preccurlyeq\overline{a}
for all

b\in{\mathcal{A}}
. Similarly, if
u
is the box above
x
, then
S(u)\succ S(x)
because
b\succ a\Leftrightarrow b\succ\overline{a}

for all
b\in{\mathcal{A}}
.

If
u
is the box to the right of
x
(
u
might be a flag box), the
only way to have
S(x)\not\preccurlyeq S^{\prime}(u)
is if
S^{\prime}(u)=T^{\prime}(u)=a

and
T(x)=a
, so
S(x)=\overline{a}
. Similarly, if
u
is the
box below
x
, the only way to have
S(x)\not\succ S^{\prime}(u)
is if

S^{\prime}(u)=T^{\prime}(u)=\overline{a}
and
T(x)=\overline{a}
, so
S(x)=a
. In either case,
c(u)=c(x)+1
, so
x<y<u
in reading order.
Hence, by the argument used previously as part of the proof that
y

is unique, it follows that
y
is not a flag box. But then
(y,u)
is
an attacking pair with
|T^{\prime}(y)|=|T^{\prime}(u)|
, contrary to the choice of

x
.

Since
|S|=|T|
, the tableau
S
again violates the non-attacking
condition, and the distinguished attacking pair in the construction of

\Psi(S)
is the same pair
(x,y)
as for
T
. Hence
\Psi(S)=T
,
i.e.,
\Psi
is an involution.

Clearly,
x^{|S|}=x^{|T|}
. Exchanging
S
and
T
if needed, we
can assume that
T(x)=a
and
S(x)=\overline{a}
. Then
(x,y)
is
an inversion in
S
but not in
T
. We have already shown that if

(x,y^{\prime})
is another attacking pair with
y\not=y^{\prime}
, then
|T^{\prime}(y^{\prime})|\not=a
, hence
(x,y^{\prime})
is an inversion in
S
if and only if it is
an inversion in
T
. Since
b\succ a\Leftrightarrow b\succ\overline{a}
for all
b\in{\mathcal{A}}
, we also have that any attacking
pair
(w,x)
is an inversion in
S
if and only if it is an inversion
in
T
. The same obviously holds for any attacking pair not
containing
x
. Thus,
S
and
T
have the same inversions except for

(x,y)
, giving
\inv(S)=\inv(T)+1
. Finally, since we changed
one entry from positive to negative,
m(S)=m(T)+1
. It follows that

(-t)^{m(S)}t^{-\inv(S)}x^{|S|}=-(-t)^{m(T)}t^{-\inv(T)}x^{|T|}
, so the terms for
S
and
T
in (
248
)
cancel.
∎

Example 6.7.10
.

In Example
6.7.4
, the only non-attacking tableau
in (
252
) is the one in the middle. The terms
for the other two tableaux cancel, illustrating
Proposition
6.7.9
.

7.
Nonsymmetric Macdonald polynomials

In this section, we show that Conjecture
6.6.1

implies an atom positivity conjecture for nonsymmetric plethystic
transformations applied to Knop’s integral form nonsymmetric Macdonald
polynomials
{\mathcal{E}}_{\mu}(x;q,t)
, generalizing positivity of the
Kostka-Macdonald coefficients
K_{\lambda,\mu}(q,t)
for symmetric
Macdonald polynomials. Our positivity conjecture requires that we
first stabilize
{\mathcal{E}}_{\mu}(x;q,t)
to be symmetric in extra
variables, as do other nonsymmetric generalizations of
K_{\lambda,\mu}(q,t)
positivity conjectured by Knop
[
27
]
and
Lapointe
[
29
]
. In the present context, we can explain
the need for stabilization as emerging naturally from the way
nonsymmetric Macdonald polynomials connect with flagged LLT
polynomials and nonsymmetric plethysm. In subsequent papers, we will
apply the methods of this paper to obtain new results on the
conjectures in
[
27
]
and
[
29
]
.

Throughout this section we will work over the field
{\mathbbm{k}}={\mathbb{Q}}(q,t)
.

7.1.
Integral forms

We define the
row diagram

{\boldsymbol{\nu}}=\dg(\mu)
of
\mu\in{\mathbb{N}}^{m}
to be the tuple of single rows
\nu^{(i)}=(0/-\mu_{m+1-i})

with end content
0
and length
\mu_{m+1-i}
. Since our convention
is to number rows from bottom to top,
\dg(\mu)
has row lengths

\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{m}
from top to bottom. The
arm
of a
box
u\in\dg(\mu)
of content
-c
in a row of length
\mu_{i}
is
the number
a(u)=\mu_{i}-c
of boxes to the left of
u
in the same
row. The
leg

l(u)
is the number of indices
j<i
such that
c\leq\mu_{j}\leq\mu_{i}
plus the number of indices
j>i
such that

c\leq\mu_{j}+1\leq\mu_{i}
.

A more visual way to describe the leg of a box is as follows. Define
the
hand
of a row of length
\mu_{i}
to be the box of content

-(\mu_{i}+1)
immediately to its left. If
v
is the hand in the
row of
u
, then
l(u)
is the number of hands strictly between
v

and
u
in the content/row reading order.

Example 7.1.1
.

The row diagram of
\mu=(2,1,4,4,0,1,4)
is shown below, with hands
indicated by circles. For the box labeled
u
, the arm
a(u)=2

counts the boxes marked
*
, and the leg
l(u)=3
counts the shaded
hands.

(259)

u
*
*

After rotating the picture 180 degrees, our definitions of diagram,
arms, and legs are the same as in Knop
[
26
]
.

Definition 7.1.2

(
[
26
]
)
.

The
integral form nonsymmetric Macdonald polynomials
are

(260)

{\mathcal{E}}_{\mu}(x;q,t)=\bigl(\prod\nolimits_{\,u\in\dg(\mu)}(1-q^{a(u)+1}\,t^{l(u)+1})\bigr)\,E_{\mu}(x;q,t).

By
[
26
, Corollary 5.2]
,
{\mathcal{E}}_{\mu}(x;q,t)
has
coefficients in
{\mathbb{Z}}[q,t]
, whence the term
integral form
. A
combinatorial formula for
{\mathcal{E}}_{\mu}(x;q,t)
was given in

[
21
, Corollary 3.5.2]
. We will state it here in terms of
fillings of the row diagram
\dg(\mu)
, adjusting for the fact
that
[
21
]
uses column diagrams, and swaps the terms ‘arm’
and ‘leg’ versus the usage here and in
[
26
]
.

Fix
\mu\in{\mathbb{N}}^{m}
, and regard
\dg(\mu)
as a tuple of
ragged-right diagrams, with a flag box of content
0
in each row, as
in Definition
6.1.6
. Let
\dg(\mu)^{\prime}
be the
extended tuple including the flag boxes. Given a filling
U\colon\dg(\mu)\rightarrow[m]
with entries in
[m]=\{1,2,\ldots,m\}
, we
define the
extended filling

U^{\prime}:\dg(\mu)^{\prime}\rightarrow[m]
as
in (
192
), taking the fixed entries in the flag boxes to
be
m,\ldots,1
, so that the row with
i
in its flag box has length

\mu_{i}
.

We define attacking pairs for
\dg(\mu)
as in
Definition
6.4.8
—thus, the first box in an attacking
pair must be in
\dg(\mu)
, but the second may be a flag box. An

attacking inversion
in
U
is an attacking pair
(x,y)
such
that
U(x)>U^{\prime}(y)
; the number of attacking inversions is denoted
\inv(U)
. A filling
U\colon\dg(\mu)\rightarrow[m]
is
non-attacking
if the extended filling
U^{\prime}
satisfies
U(x)\not=U^{\prime}(y)
for all attacking pairs
(x,y)
. These definitions are
essentially the same as in Definition
6.4.8
and
Definition
6.7.8
, except that the filling
U
is
unsigned and is not required to be a flagged tableau.

For any box
u\in\dg(\mu)
, let
e(u)\in\dg(\mu)^{\prime}
denote the
box immediately to its right in the same row; thus, if
u
has content

-c
, then
e(u)
has content
-c+1
, and is a flag box if
c=1
.
Given a filling
U\colon\dg(\mu)\rightarrow[m]
, define

(261)

\displaystyle\Des(U)

\displaystyle=\{u\in\dg(\mu)\mid U(u)>U^{\prime}(e(u))\}

(262)

\displaystyle\maj(U)

\displaystyle=\sum\nolimits_{u\in\Des(U)}(a(u)+1)

(263)

\displaystyle\coinv(U)

\displaystyle=-\inv(U)+n(\mu_{+})+\sum\nolimits_{u\in\Des(U)}l(u),

where
\mu_{+}
is the rearrangement of
\mu
to a partition, and

n(\mu_{+})=\sum_{j}(j-1)(\mu_{+})_{j}
, as in
(
7
).

We note that
\inv
and
\coinv
are defined a little differently in

[
21
]
, in such a way that direct translation into the
notation here would give

(264)

\coinv(U)=-\inv(U)+N+\sum\nolimits_{u\in\Des(U)}l(u),

where
N=|\{(i<j)\mid\mu_{i}>\mu_{j}\}|+\sum_{u\in\dg(\mu)}l(\mu)
. It is a fairly straightforward exercise to check that,
in fact,
N=n(\mu_{+})
.

Proposition 7.1.3

(
[
21
]
)
.

With notation as above, for any
\mu\in{\mathbb{N}}^{m}
, we have

(265)

{\mathcal{E}}_{\mu}(x;q,t)\,=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U\colon\dg(\mu)\rightarrow[m]\\
\text{non-attacking}\end{subarray}}\hskip-4.30554ptq^{\maj(U)}\,t^{\coinv(U)}\hskip-8.61108pt\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\dg(\mu)\\
U(u)=U^{\prime}(e(u))\end{subarray}}\hskip-12.91663pt(1-q^{a(u)+1}t^{l(u)+1})\hskip-8.61108pt\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\dg(\mu)\\
U(u)\not=U^{\prime}(e(u))\end{subarray}}\hskip-12.91663pt(1-t)\;\;x^{U}\,.

Example 7.1.4
.

For
\mu=(2,0,1)
, we display the arm and leg of each box in the row
diagram
\dg(\mu)
, the attacking pairs in the extended diagram
\dg(\mu)^{\prime}
, and all non-attacking fillings
U\colon\dg(\mu)\rightarrow[3]
, with their extensions
U^{\prime}
.

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{4.61319pt}{46.57011pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
