{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-23.83957pt}{46.57011pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-9.61319pt}{46.57011pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-9.61319pt}{3.89098pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\\
\text{arms}&\text{legs}&\text{attacking pairs}&\lx@intercol\hfil\text{non-attacking fillings}\hfil\lx@intercol\end{array}

In this example,
\mu_{+}=(2,1)
and
n(\mu_{+})=1
. For the
four non-attacking fillings
U
in the order displayed, we have
\maj(U)=0,0,1,1
;
\inv(U)=1,1,2,1
;
\sum_{u\in\Des(U)}l(u)=0,0,1,1
;
\coinv(U)=0,0,0,1
; hence,
q^{\maj(U)}t^{\coinv(U)}=1,1,q,qt
. Formula (
265
) now reads

(267)

{\mathcal{E}}_{(2,0,1)}(x;q,t)=(1-t)(1-q\,t^{2})(1-q^{2}t^{3})x_{1}^{2}x_{2}+(1-q\,t)(1-q\,t^{2})(1-q^{2}t^{3})x_{1}^{2}x_{3}\\
+q\,(1-t)^{2}(1-q^{2}t^{3})x_{1}x_{2}x_{3}+q\,t\,(1-t)(1-q\,t)(1-q^{2}t^{3})x_{1}x_{2}x_{3}.

The last two terms could be combined and simplified, but we have left
them as they are so as to keep the correspondence with non-attacking
fillings visible.

Expanding the products in (
265
) leads to an
equivalent formula expressed as a sum over signed fillings
S\colon\dg(\mu)\rightarrow[m]_{\pm}
, where
[m]_{\pm}=\{1<\overline{1}<\cdots<m<\overline{m}\}
with
([m]_{\pm})^{+}=\{1,\ldots,m\}
and
([m]_{\pm})^{-}=\{\overline{1},\ldots,\overline{m}\}
. We define a signed filling
S
to be non-attacking
if the unsigned filling
U=|S|
is non-attacking. Then, taking

\inv(S)
to be the number of attacking pairs
(x,y)
for
\dg(\mu)
such that
S(x)\succ S^{\prime}(y)
, as in
Definition
6.4.8
, we have
\inv(S)=\inv(U)
, since
the non-attacking condition
|S(x)|\not=|S^{\prime}(y)|
implies

S(x)\succ S^{\prime}(y)\Leftrightarrow U(x)>U^{\prime}(y)
.

Corollary 7.1.5
.

With the above definitions for signed fillings, for any
\mu\in{\mathbb{N}}^{m}
, we have

(268)

{\mathcal{E}}_{\mu}(x;q,t)=t^{n(\mu_{+})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\colon\dg(\mu)\rightarrow[m]_{\pm}\\
\text{non-attacking}\end{subarray}}(-t)^{m(S)}\,t^{-\inv(S)}\,x^{|S|}\,\!\!\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\dg(\mu)\\
S(u)\succ S^{\prime}(e(u))\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!q^{a(u)+1}\,t^{l(u)},

where
m(S)
is the number of negative entries in
S
.

Proof.

Letting
U=|S|
, we have
\inv(S)=\inv(U)
, and
S(u)\succ S^{\prime}(e(u))
for all
u\in\Des(U)
. We also have
S(u)\preccurlyeq S^{\prime}(e(u))
if
U(u)<U^{\prime}(e(u))
—that is, if
u\not\in\Des(U)
and

U(u)\not=U^{\prime}(e(u))
. Hence, the term for
S
in
(
268
) factors as

(269)

\biggl(t^{n(\mu_{+})}\,t^{-\inv(U)}\,x^{U}\,\!\prod_{u\in\Des(U)}q^{a(u)+1}\,t^{l(u)}\biggr)\times\biggl((-t)^{m(S)}\!\!\!\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\dg(\mu)\\
U(u)=U^{\prime}(e(u))\\
S(u)\succ S^{\prime}(e(u))\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\,q^{a(u)+1}\,t^{l(u)}\biggr)\,.

The first factor is equal to
q^{\maj(U)}\,t^{\coinv(U)}\,x^{U}
.
When
U(u)=U^{\prime}(e(u))
, we have
S(u)\succ S^{\prime}(e(u))
if and only if

S(u)
is negative. Hence, if
S(u)
is positive, the contribution
from
u
in the second factor is
1
, and if is
S(u)
is negative, it
is either
-t
, if
U(u)\not=U^{\prime}(e(u))
, or
-q^{a(u)+1}t^{a(u)+1}
,
if
U(u)=U^{\prime}(e(u))
. Summing this factor over all choices of signs
for a fixed
|S|=U
therefore gives the product

(270)

\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\dg(\mu)\\
U(u)=U^{\prime}(e(u))\end{subarray}}\hskip-12.91663pt(1-q^{a(u)+1}t^{l(u)+1})\hskip-12.91663pt\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\dg(\mu)\\
U(u)\not=U^{\prime}(e(u))\end{subarray}}\hskip-12.91663pt(1-t)

in the term for
U
on the right hand side of (
265
).
∎

Later we will need the following property of the integral forms.

Proposition 7.1.6
.

For any
\mu\in{\mathbb{N}}^{m}
and
1\leq i<m
, the difference
{\mathcal{E}}_{s_{i}\mu}(x;q,t)-{\mathcal{E}}_{\mu}(x;q,t)
is
T_{i}
antisymmetric,
i.e.,

(271)

(T_{i}+1)({\mathcal{E}}_{s_{i}\mu}(x;q,t)-{\mathcal{E}}_{\mu}(x;q,t))=0.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we can assume that
\mu_{i}>\mu_{i+1}
.
For any box
u\in\dg(\mu)
, let
\widehat{u}\in\dg(s_{i}\mu)
be the
box corresponding to
u
after switching rows
m-i+1
and
m-i
(i.e.,
the rows of lengths
\mu_{i}
and
\mu_{i+1}
). The only factor

(1-q^{a(u)+1}t^{l(u)+1})
in (
260
) for
{\mathcal{E}}_{\mu}(x;q,t)
that differs from the corresponding factor
(1-q^{a(\widehat{u})+1}t^{l(\widehat{u})+1})
for
{\mathcal{E}}_{s_{i}\mu}(x;q,t)
is the one for
the box
u
of content
-(\mu_{i+1}+1)
in row
m-i+1
. For this
box, we have
a(u)=a(\widehat{u})=\mu_{i}-\mu_{i+1}-1
,

l(u)=w_{\mu}(i)-w_{\mu}(i+1)
, where
w_{\mu}
is as in
(
172
), and
l(\widehat{u})=l(u)-1
, because
l(u)

counts a hand in row
m-i
which is not counted in
l(\widehat{u})
. Hence
(
271
) is equivalent to

(272)

(T_{i}+1)\left(E_{s_{i}\mu}(x;q,t)-\frac{1-q^{\mu_{i}-\mu_{i+1}}t^{w_{\mu}(i)-w_{\mu}(i+1)+1}}{1-q^{\mu_{i}-\mu_{i+1}}t^{w_{\mu}(i)-w_{\mu}(i+1)}}E_{\mu}(x;q,t)\right)=0.

Using (
172
), this reduces to
(T_{i}+1)(T_{i}-t)E_{\mu}(x;q,t)=0
.
∎

7.2.
Flagged LLT formula for nonsymmetric Macdonald
polynomials

As noted in
[
20
]
, the combinatorial formula given there
for symmetric Macdonald polynomials can be interpreted as an expansion
in terms of symmetric LLT polynomials. We now explain how to
interpret the combinatorial formula in
[
21
]
for integral
form nonsymmetric Macdonald polynomials as an expansion in terms of
flagged LLT polynomials.

A
ribbon
is a connected skew diagram containing no
2\times 2

square—equivalently, containing no two boxes of the same content.
An
augmented ribbon
is a ragged-right diagram
\nu
such that
the flag box in the first row has content
0
, and this box and
\nu

form a ribbon. We call the flag box in the first row the
augmentation box
, and let
\nu_{\circ}
denote the ribbon with the
augmentation box included. If
|\nu|=s
, the boxes in
\nu_{\circ}

have contents
0,-1,\ldots,-s
from southeast to northwest, starting
with the augmentation box. Each pair of boxes with consecutive
contents forms a horizontal or vertical domino.

The only possible overhanging box in an augmented ribbon
\nu
is the
box of content
-1
, which may form a vertical domino with the
augmentation box. If the box of content
-1
and the augmentation box
form a horizontal domino, or if
\nu
is empty, then
\nu
is an
ordinary skew diagram. Two examples of size
|\nu|=6
are shown
here, with augmentation box indicated by
\bullet
. Each one has
four rows. The first is an ordinary skew diagram; the second is
strictly ragged-right, with first row
0/0
.

(273)

If
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}=(\nu^{(1)},\ldots,\nu^{(m)})
is a tuple of augmented
ribbons, we have
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}\subset{\boldsymbol{\nu}}_{{\boldsymbol{\circ}}}\subseteq{\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{\prime}
, where
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{\prime}
is the usual extended diagram containing
all flag boxes, and
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}_{{\boldsymbol{\circ}}}
is the tuple of ribbons
\nu^{(i)}_{\,\circ}
containing the augmentation boxes. Let
\mu^{\op}=(\mu_{m},\ldots,\mu_{1})=(|\nu^{(1)}|,\ldots,|\nu^{(m)}|)
. If all the ribbons
\nu^{(i)}_{\,\circ}
are
horizontal, then
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}=\dg(\mu)
is the row diagram of
\mu
.
In general, to every box
u\in{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
, there corresponds a unique
box
\widehat{u}
of the same content as
u
in the corresponding row of

\dg(\mu)
. We generalize the definition of arms and legs from row
diagrams to augmented ribbon tuples by setting
a(u)=a(\widehat{u})
,

l(u)=l(\widehat{u})
. Thus,
a(u)
is the number of boxes northwest of

u
in its ribbon, and if we adjoin a ‘hand’ to each ribbon, of
content
-(\mu_{i}+1)
for a ribbon of length
\mu_{i}
, then
l(u)

is again the number of hands strictly between
v
and
u
in the
content/row reading order, where
v
is the hand attached to the
ribbon containing
u
.

By Lemma
6.1.7
(a), if
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}=(\nu^{(1)},\ldots,\nu^{(m)})
is a tuple of ragged-right skew shapes such that the
first row of each
\nu^{(i)}
has end content zero, then there exists
a compatible permutation
\sigma
that assigns any permutation of the
flag numbers
1,\ldots,m
to the flag boxes in these rows. For a
tuple of augmented ribbons, these flag boxes are the augmentation
boxes.

Theorem 7.2.1
.

The integral form nonsymmetric Macdonald polynomials are given in
terms of flagged LLT polynomials by

(274)

{\mathcal{E}}_{(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{m})}(x;q,t)=t^{n(\mu_{+})}\sum_{({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma)\in R(\mu)}\bigl(\prod\nolimits_{\hbox to9.39pt{\vbox to14.71pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.14055pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}{}{
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{
}{
}{
}{
}{
}{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 0 0 L 9.84 0 M 0 9.84 L 9.84 9.84 M 0 19.68 L 9.84 19.68 M 0 0 L 0 19.69 M 9.84 0 L 9.84 19.69 M 9.84 19.69}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{1.19257pt}{9.16284pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}q^{a(u)+1}\,t^{l(u)}\bigr)\,{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}^{-}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m},0,\ldots,0;\,t^{-1})\,,

where
R(\mu)
contains one pair
({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma)
for each tuple

{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
of augmented ribbons of lengths
|\nu^{(i)}|=(\mu^{\op})_{i}=\mu_{m+1-i}
, and
\sigma
assigns flag numbers

m,\ldots,2,1
to the augmentation boxes. The product of arm and leg
factors
q^{a(u)+1}\,t^{l(u)}
in each term is over the north boxes

u
in all vertical dominoes in
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}_{{\boldsymbol{\circ}}}
.

Proof.

Throughout the argument,
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
denotes a tuple of augmented
ribbons of sizes
\mu^{\op}
. By
Proposition
6.7.9
,
{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}^{-}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{l};t^{-1})
is the sum of
(-t)^{m(T)}\,t^{-\inv(T)}\,x^{|T|}
over non-attacking flagged tableaux
T\in\FSST({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma,{\mathcal{A}}^{b}_{\pm})
, where
{\mathcal{A}}^{b}_{\pm}

is defined by (
247
) with flag bounds

b_{i}=i
. Setting
x_{i}=0
for
i>m
gives the sum of the terms
for tableaux
T
with entries in
[m]_{\pm}=\{1,\overline{1},\ldots,m,\overline{m}\}
.

If
z\in{\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{\prime}\setminus{\boldsymbol{\nu}}_{{\boldsymbol{\circ}}}
is a flag box which
is not an augmentation box, then no box overhangs
z
, and the flag
bound in
z
is greater than
m
. These ‘irrelevant’ flag boxes
impose no constraint on tableaux
T
with entries in
[m]_{\pm}
.
They are also irrelevant in that no attacking pair
(u,z)
with
z
as
the second box can be an attacking inversion, or violate the
non-attacking condition. In particular,
{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}^{-}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m},0,\ldots,0;t^{-1})
is independent of
the specific choice of
\sigma
assigning flag numbers
m,\ldots,1

to the augmentation boxes.

Given
T\colon{\boldsymbol{\nu}}\rightarrow[m]_{\pm}
, let
\Phi(T)
be the
signed filling
S\colon\dg(\mu)\rightarrow[m]_{\pm}
such that

S(\widehat{u})=T(u)
for each box
u\in{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
, where
\widehat{u}
is the
box of the same content as
u
in the row of
\dg(\mu)

corresponding to the component of
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
containing
u
. Note
that we also have
S^{\prime}(\widehat{u})=T^{\prime}(u)
for the fixed flag bound
entries
m,\ldots,1
in the augmentation boxes
u\in{\boldsymbol{\nu}}_{{\boldsymbol{\circ}}}
and corresponding flag boxes
\widehat{u}\in\dg(\mu)^{\prime}
. Setting
aside the non-attacking condition for the moment, the only constraints
for a filling
T\colon{\boldsymbol{\nu}}\rightarrow[m]_{\pm}
to be a flagged
tableau are that for every domino
\{u,v\}
in
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}_{{\boldsymbol{\circ}}}
,
with
v
the box of larger content, i.e., the south or east box, we
have
T^{\prime}(u)\preccurlyeq T^{\prime}(v)
if
\{u,v\}
is a horizontal domino, and

T^{\prime}(u)\succ T^{\prime}(v)
if it is vertical. It follows that every signed
filling
S\colon\dg(\mu)\rightarrow[m]_{\pm}
is
\Phi(T)
for a
unique flagged tableau
T\colon{\boldsymbol{\nu}}\rightarrow[m]_{\pm}
on a
unique augmented ribbon tuple
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
: namely, the ribbon tuple
such that the domino
\{u,v\}
corresponding to each pair
\{\widehat{u},e(\widehat{u})\}
in
\dg(\mu)^{\prime}
is horizontal if
S(\widehat{u})\preccurlyeq S^{\prime}(e(\widehat{u}))
, and vertical if
S(\widehat{u})\succ S^{\prime}(e(\widehat{u}))
.

From the definitions, attacking pairs
(u,v)
for
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
such that

v\in{\boldsymbol{\nu}}_{{\boldsymbol{\circ}}}
correspond to attacking pairs
(\widehat{u},\widehat{v})
for
\dg(\mu)
. It follows that
T
is non-attacking if and only
if
S=\Phi(T)
is non-attacking, and that
\inv(T)=\inv(S)
.
Clearly,
x^{|T|}=x^{|S|}
. By definition,
a(u)=a(\widehat{u})
and

l(u)=l(\widehat{u})
for all
u
in
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
. Since
u\in{\boldsymbol{\nu}}

is the north box in a vertical domino if and only if
S(\widehat{u})\succ S^{\prime}(e(\widehat{u}))
, the product
\prod_{u\in\dg(\mu):S(u)\succ S^{\prime}(e(u))}q^{a(u)+1}\,t^{l(u)}
in (
268
) is equal to
the product
\prod_{\resizebox{327880}{}{\hbox to9.39pt{\vbox to14.71pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.14055pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}{}{
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{
}{
}{
}{
}{
}{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 0 0 L 9.84 0 M 0 9.84 L 9.84 9.84 M 0 19.68 L 9.84 19.68 M 0 0 L 0 19.69 M 9.84 0 L 9.84 19.69 M 9.84 19.69}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{1.19257pt}{9.16284pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}q^{a(u)+1}\,t^{l(u)}
in (
274
) for
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
. Hence, after expanding
each
{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}^{-}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m},0,\ldots,0;t^{-1})
in (
274
) as a sum over non-attacking flagged
tableaux, the right hand sides of (
268
) and
(
274
) are equal term by term.
∎

{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}_{5},\sigma_{5}}^{-}(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},0;t^{-1})&=&(-t)\,t^{-1}x_{1}^{2}x_{2}&+&(-t)^{2}\,t^{-1}\,x_{1}^{2}x_{2}&+&(-t)\,t^{-1}x_{1}^{2}x_{3}\,.\end{array}

The map
\Phi
in the proof of Theorem
7.2.1
sends these
flagged tableaux to the non-attacking signed fillings
S
of
\dg(\mu)
shown here.

In Example
6.7.4
, the four terms in
(
267
) correspond to unsigned fillings

U
of
\dg(\mu)
. Multiplying out the products in
(
267
) gives eight terms for each
U
,
corresponding as in Corollary
7.1.5
to signed fillings

S
such that
|S|=U
. The first two signed fillings in
(
278
) correspond in this way to
(-q^{2}t^{3})(1-t)\,x_{1}^{2}x_{2}
from the first term in
(
267
), and the third to
-q^{2}t^{3}\,x_{1}^{2}x_{3}
from the second term in
(
267
). As the reader can verify, we get
the same terms by multiplying the expression for
{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}_{5},\sigma_{5}}^{-}(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},0;t^{-1})
in (
277
) by
the corresponding factor
q^{2}t^{3}
from
(
275
).

One useful consequence of Theorem
7.2.1
is that it permits
us to generalize formulas (
265
) and (
274
)
for
{\mathcal{E}}_{\mu}(x;q,t)
to formulas for
T_{w}\,{\mathcal{E}}_{\mu}(x;q,t)
.

Corollary 7.2.3
.

Let
\mu\in{\mathbb{N}}^{m}
and
w\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{m}
be given.

(a) Modify notation in Proposition
7.1.3
by taking
the extended filling
U^{\prime}
for
U\colon\dg(\mu)\rightarrow[m]
to
have
w(m),\ldots,w(1)
instead of
m,\ldots,1
in the flag boxes, and
redefining
\coinv(U)
accordingly—i.e., using the modified
U^{\prime}
to
define both
\inv(U)
and
\Des(U)
in (
263
). Then

(279)

T_{w}\,{\mathcal{E}}_{\mu}(x;q,t)=t^{\ell(w)}\hskip-8.61108pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U\colon\dg(\mu)\rightarrow[m]\\
\text{non-attacking}\end{subarray}}\hskip-6.45831ptq^{\maj(U)}\,t^{\coinv(U)}\hskip-12.91663pt\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\dg(\mu)\\
U^{\prime}(u)=U^{\prime}(e(u))\end{subarray}}\hskip-12.91663pt(1-q^{a(u)+1}t^{l(u)+1})\hskip-12.91663pt\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\dg(\mu)\\
U^{\prime}(u)\not=U^{\prime}(e(u))\end{subarray}}\hskip-12.91663pt(1-t)\;x^{U}\,.

(b) Define
R_{w}(\mu)
similarly to
R(\mu)
in Theorem
7.2.1
, but
choose each pair
({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma)
so that the flag numbers in
the augmentation boxes of
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
are
w(m),\ldots,w(1)
instead of

m,\ldots,1
. Then

(280)

T_{w}\,{\mathcal{E}}_{\mu}(x;q,t)=t^{\ell(w)+n(\mu_{+})}\sum_{({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma)\in R_{w}(\mu)}\bigl(\prod\nolimits_{\hbox to9.39pt{\vbox to14.71pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.14055pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}{}{
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{
}{
}{
}{
}{
}{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 0 0 L 9.84 0 M 0 9.84 L 9.84 9.84 M 0 19.68 L 9.84 19.68 M 0 0 L 0 19.69 M 9.84 0 L 9.84 19.69 M 9.84 19.69}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{1.19257pt}{9.16284pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}q^{a(u)+1}\,t^{l(u)}\bigr)\,{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}^{-}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m},0,\ldots,0;\,t^{-1})\,.

Proof.

Since
T_{w}
commutes with setting
x_{i}=0
for
i>m
, part (b)
follows from Theorem
7.2.1
and
Proposition
6.7.6
. For part (a), we need to see that
the right hand sides of (
279
) and
(
280
) become equal term by term after multiplying
out the products in (
279
), and expressing
each
{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}^{-}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m},0,\ldots,0;\,t^{-1})
in (
280
) as a sum over non-attacking
flagged tableaux. When
w
is the identity, this was shown in the
proofs of Corollary
7.1.5
and Theorem
7.2.1
.
The argument goes through essentially unaltered with flag bounds

w(m),\ldots,w(1)
instead of
m,\ldots,1
in the flag boxes of
\dg(\mu)
and the augmentation boxes of
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
.
∎

Remark 7.2.4
.

In formulas (
265
), (
268
),
(
274
), and
(
279
)–(
280
), each term in
the sum on the right hand side clearly has coefficients in
{\mathbb{Z}}[q,t^{\pm 1}]
, but it is not obvious
a priori
that the
individual terms have coefficients
{\mathbb{Z}}[q,t]
, even though the sum as
a whole does.

The next lemma implies that every term on the right hand side of
(
274
) and (
280
) has coefficients in
{\mathbb{Z}}[q,t]
, and more generally that in these formulas, the full flagged
LLT term

(281)

t^{\ell(w)+n(\mu_{+})}\bigl(\prod\nolimits_{\hbox to9.39pt{\vbox to14.71pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.14055pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}{}{
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{
}{
}{
}{
}{
}{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 0 0 L 9.84 0 M 0 9.84 L 9.84 9.84 M 0 19.68 L 9.84 19.68 M 0 0 L 0 19.69 M 9.84 0 L 9.84 19.69 M 9.84 19.69}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{1.19257pt}{9.16284pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}q^{a(u)+1}\,t^{l(u)}\bigr)\,{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{l};\,t^{-1})

has coefficients in
{\mathbb{Z}}[q,t]
even before specializing to
{\mathcal{G}}^{-}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m},0,\ldots,0;\,t^{-1})

Since the proofs of Theorem
7.2.1
and
Corollaries
7.1.5
and
7.2.3
equate
individual terms, it follows that every term on the right hand sides
of (
265
), (
268
), and
(
279
) also has coefficients in
{\mathbb{Z}}[q,t]
.
In particular, this implies that the modified definition of
\coinv(U)
involving
w
in (
279
) satisfies
\coinv(U)+l(w)\geq 0
. When
w
is the identity, this was also shown in

[
21
, Proposition 3.6.2]
.

Lemma 7.2.5
.

Let
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}=(\nu^{(1)},\ldots,\nu^{(m)})
be a tuple of
augmented ribbons of lengths
|\nu^{(i)}|=(\mu^{\op})_{i}
, and
let
\sigma
be a compatible permutation such that the flag numbers
in the augmentation boxes are
w(m),\ldots,w(1)
, where
w\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{m}
. Then, for any signed alphabet
{\mathcal{A}}
, every tableau
T\in\FSST({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma,{\mathcal{A}})
satisfies
\inv(T)\leq n(\mu_{+})+\ell(w)+\sum_{\resizebox{327880}{}{\hbox to9.39pt{\vbox to14.71pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.14055pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}{}{
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{
}{
}{
}{
}{
}{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 0 0 L 9.84 0 M 0 9.84 L 9.84 9.84 M 0 19.68 L 9.84 19.68 M 0 0 L 0 19.69 M 9.84 0 L 9.84 19.69 M 9.84 19.69}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{1.19257pt}{9.16284pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}l(u)
, where the sum
is over the north boxes
u
in all vertical dominoes in
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}_{{\boldsymbol{\circ}}}
.

Proof.

Let
I
be the set of all pairs
(x,y)
in
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}_{{\boldsymbol{\circ}}}
with

c(y)=c(x)
and
i<j
, where
x\in\nu_{\,\circ}^{(i)}
,
y\in\nu_{\,\circ}^{(j)}
. The number of such pairs with content
c(y)=c(x)<0
, i.e., with
x,y\in{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
, is
n(\mu_{+})
. In the
pairs with content
c(y)=c(x)=0
,
x
and
y
are augmentation
boxes. Let
T^{\prime}
be the extended tableau in (
192
),
where (as is always possible) we take the letters
b_{j}
to be
strictly increasing. Then the number of pairs
(x,y)\in I
with
content
c(x)=c(y)=0
and
T^{\prime}(x)\preccurlyeq T^{\prime}(y)
is
l(w)
.

It was shown in the proof of Lemma
6.5.5
that if

(x,y)
is an attacking inversion with
c(y)=c(x)
, then
y
is not
a flag box. Hence the attacking inversions with
c(y)=c(x)
for
T

are precisely the pairs
(x,y)\in I
such that
x,y\in{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
and

T(x)\succ T(y)
. Let
\inv_{1}(T)
be the number of these pairs,
and let
g=|H|
, where
H=\{(x,y)\in I\mid T^{\prime}(x)\preccurlyeq T^{\prime}(y)\}
. By the preceding considerations,
\inv_{1}(T)+g=n(\mu_{+})+l(w)
. It therefore remains to show that
\inv_{2}(T)\leq g+\sum_{\resizebox{327880}{}{\hbox to9.39pt{\vbox to14.71pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.14055pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}{}{
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{
}{
}{
}{
}{
}{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 0 0 L 9.84 0 M 0 9.84 L 9.84 9.84 M 0 19.68 L 9.84 19.68 M 0 0 L 0 19.69 M 9.84 0 L 9.84 19.69 M 9.84 19.69}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{1.19257pt}{9.16284pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}l(u)
, where
\inv_{2}(T)
is
the number of attacking inversions
(x,y)
for
T
with
c(y)=c(x)+1
and
i>j
, where
x\in\nu^{(i)}
and
y\in\nu^{\prime}\,{}^{(j)}
.
Let
J
be the set of attacking inversions
(x,y)
of this latter
type.

Another way to describe the leg of a box
u\in{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
, as in

[
26
, §5]
or
[
28
, §4]
, is that
l(u)
is the
number of boxes
v\in{\boldsymbol{\nu}}_{{\boldsymbol{\circ}}}
such that we either have

u\in\nu^{(j)}
,
v\in\nu_{\,\circ}^{(i)}
,
|\nu^{(j)}|\geq|\nu^{(i)}|
, and
c(v)=c(u)
, or we have
u\in\nu^{(i)}
,
v\in\nu_{\,\circ}^{(j)}
,
|\nu^{(j)}|<|\nu^{(i)}|
, and
c(v)=c(u)+1
, where
i>j
in either case. Let
K
be the set of such pairs

(u,v)
, where
u
is the north box in a vertical domino in
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}_{{\boldsymbol{\circ}}}
, so that
|K|=\sum_{\resizebox{327880}{}{\hbox to9.39pt{\vbox to14.71pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.14055pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}{}{
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{
}{
}{
}{
}{
}{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 0 0 L 9.84 0 M 0 9.84 L 9.84 9.84 M 0 19.68 L 9.84 19.68 M 0 0 L 0 19.69 M 9.84 0 L 9.84 19.69 M 9.84 19.69}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{1.19257pt}{9.16284pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}l(u)
. We will show
that
|J|\leq|H|+|K|
, as desired, by constructing an injective map

(x,y)\mapsto(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\colon J\hookrightarrow H\coprod K
.

Given
(x,y)\in J
with
x\in\nu^{(i)}
and
y\in\nu^{\prime}\,{}^{(j)}
,
where
i>j
, we consider four cases. Suppose first that
|\nu^{(j)}|\geq|\nu^{(i)}|
. Then there is a unique box
u\in\nu^{(j)}
with
content
c(u)=c(x)
, which forms a domino with
y
. Case (1a) is
that
\{u,y\}
is a horizontal domino. Then
(u,x)\in H
, since

T(u)\preccurlyeq T^{\prime}(y)\prec T(x)
. Case (1b) is that
\{u,y\}
is a
vertical domino. Then
(u,x)\in K
, since
|\nu^{(j)}|\geq|\nu^{(i)}|
. In either case, we set
(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})=(u,x)
, considered as an
element of
H
or
K
, respectively.

Otherwise,
|\nu^{(j)}|<|\nu^{(i)}|
. Since
c(x)<0
, there is a
unique box
z\in\nu_{\,\circ}^{(i)}
with content
c(z)=c(y)
,
which forms a domino with
x
. Case (2a) is that
\{x,z\}
is a
horizontal domino. Then
(y,z)\in H
, since
T^{\prime}(y)\prec T(x)\preccurlyeq T(z)
. In this case we set
(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})=(y,z)
. Case (2b) is that

\{x,z\}
is a vertical domino. Then
(x,y)\in K
, since
|\nu^{(j)}|<|\nu^{(i)}|
, and we set
(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})=(x,y)
, considered as an
element of
K
.

Since we always map
(x,y)
to a pair
(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})
in the same two
components of
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}_{{\boldsymbol{\circ}}}
as
(x,y)
itself,
(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})

determines
i
and
j
. Then
(x,y)
belongs to Case (1a) or (1b) if

|\nu^{(j)}|\geq|\nu^{(i)}|
, and Case (2a) or (2b) if
|\nu^{(j)}|<|\nu^{(i)}|
. By definition, we record whether
(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})

is in
H
or
K
, so we can further distinguish Case (1a) from (1b)
and Case (2a) from (2b). Once we know which case
(x,y)
is in, it is
clear that we can recover
(x,y)
from
(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})
. Hence the
map we have constructed is injective.
∎

7.3.
Stabilized nonsymmetric Macdonald polynomials

There are two natural ways to construct a polynomial symmetric in some
block of variables from a nonsymmetric Macdonald polynomial. The
first—
specialization
—uses the fact that if
\mu_{i}=\mu_{i+1}
, then
{\mathcal{E}}_{\mu}(x;q,t)
is symmetric in
x_{i},x_{i+1}

(§
5.4
). Hence, given
\eta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{r}
,
\lambda\in{\mathbb{N}}^{k}
, and
\kappa\in{\mathbb{N}}^{s}
, the polynomial

(282)

{\mathcal{E}}_{(\eta;\,0^{n};\,\lambda;\,\kappa)}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r},y_{1},\ldots,y_{n},0^{k},z_{1},\ldots,z_{s};\,q,t)

is symmetric in new variables
y_{1},\ldots,y_{n}
which replace the
variables
x_{i}
indexed by the positions of
\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{k}
. By specializing the integral form
{\mathcal{E}}_{\mu}(x;q,t)
rather than
E_{\mu}(x;q,t)
, we get a polynomial
with coefficients in
{\mathbb{Z}}[q,t]
. We insert
0^{n}
before
\lambda

in
(\eta;\,0^{n};\,\lambda;\,\kappa)
, and not after, because

{\mathcal{E}}_{(\eta;\,\lambda;\,0^{n};\,\kappa)}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r},0^{k},y_{1},\ldots,y_{n},z_{1},\ldots,z_{s};\,q,t)=0
if
\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{r},\lambda_{1}>0
.

The second construction—
symmetrization
—uses the symmetric
linear combination of nonsymmetric Macdonald polynomials in
(
174
)–(
175
), for the
Young subgroup
{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}={\mathfrak{S}}_{[i,j]}
which permutes a
block of indices. In particular, given
\eta
,
\lambda
,
\kappa

as above, there is, up to a scalar factor, a unique polynomial of the
form

(283)

\sum_{v}c_{v}\,E_{(\eta;\,v(0^{n};\,\lambda);\,\kappa)}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r},y_{1},\ldots,y_{n+k},z_{1},\ldots,z_{s};q,t)

symmetric in
y_{1},\ldots,y_{n+k}
, where
v(0^{n};\,\lambda)

varies over permutations of
(0^{n};\,\lambda)
. The monically
normalized form of (
283
), in which the leading
term
x^{\eta}\,m_{\lambda_{+}}(y)z^{\kappa}
has coefficient

1
, is given more explicitly by

(284)

\bigl(\sum\nolimits_{w\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{[r+1,r+n+k]}/{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{s}}}}T_{w}\,E_{(\eta;\,\lambda_{+};\,0^{n};\,\kappa)}(x;q,t)\bigr)\big|_{x\mapsto(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r},y_{1},\ldots,y_{n+k},z_{1},\ldots,z_{s})}\,,

where
{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{s}}}=\Stab_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{[r+1,r+n+k]}}((\eta;\,\lambda_{+};\,0^{n};\,\kappa))
, and
{\mathfrak{S}}_{[r+1,r+n+k]}/{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbf{s}}}
is the set of minimal coset representatives. We will
show below that the specialization (
282
) and
symmetrization (
284
) each converge in a
suitable sense to a symmetric function in
Y=y_{1},y_{2},\cdots
as

n\rightarrow\infty
, and that, up to a scalar factor, the limit is
the same for either construction.

Fixing nonsymmetric variables
x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}
and taking the
symmetric limit in the rest—i.e., taking
\kappa
empty in
(
282
)–(
284
)—yields
a basis of
right stabilized
Macdonald polynomials, indexed by
pairs
\eta,\lambda
, where
\eta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{r}
and
\lambda
is a
partition, for the algebra
{\mathbbm{k}}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}]\otimes\Lambda(Y)
of
r
-nonsymmetric polynomials
.

Alternatively, taking
\eta
empty yields a basis for
{\mathbbm{k}}[z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}]\otimes\Lambda(Y)
consisting of
left
stabilized
Macdonald polynomials. Although left stabilization is
simpler in some ways, right stabilization is more general, because the
rotation identity (
173
) can be used to transform the left
stabilized basis (for
s=r
) into a subset of the right stabilized
basis. Our results and conjectures in §§
7.4
–
7.6
cover the more general right
stabilized case.

Both flavors of stabilized Macdonald polynomials have appeared before
in the literature, and parts of the unified treatment we will present
here are already known. Remark
7.3.9
, below,
summarizes connections with previous results.

We now develop properties of the above constructions in detail,
starting with specialization, which we view as the preferred
construction because it leads directly to stabilized integral forms.

Analogous to the notation in (
246
), given flagged LLT data

{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma
of length
l
, define

(285)

{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}^{-}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r},Y,z_{1},\ldots,z_{l-r};\,t^{-1})\\
={\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}[x_{1},\,x_{2}-t\,x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}-t\,x_{r-1},\\
z_{1}+(1-t)Y-t\,x_{r},\,z_{2}-t\,z_{1},\ldots,z_{l-r}-t\,z_{l-r-1};\,t^{-1}],

a polynomial in
x_{1},\ldots,x_{r},z_{1},\ldots,z_{l-r}
and
symmetric function in
Y
. Evaluated in a finite plethystic alphabet

Y=y_{1}+\cdots+y_{n}
, this becomes the signed flagged LLT
polynomial in (
245
), in variables

x_{1},\ldots,x_{r},y_{1},\ldots,y_{n},z_{1},\ldots,z_{l-r}
, for flag
bounds
b_{i}=i
for
i\leq r
,
b_{i}=n+i
for
i>r
.

Next, we take a variant of the formula in Theorem
7.2.1
as
our definition of what will turn out to be the limit of the
specialized integral forms in (
282
) as

n\rightarrow\infty

Definition 7.3.1
.

Given
\eta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{r}
,
\lambda\in{\mathbb{N}}^{k}
and
\kappa\in{\mathbb{N}}^{s}
, the
partially symmetric integral form Macdonald
polynomial

J_{\eta|\lambda|\kappa}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r},Y,z_{1},\ldots,z_{s};\,q,t)
is given by

(286)

J_{\eta|\lambda|\kappa}(x,Y,z;\,q,t)=\\
t^{n(\mu_{+})}\sum_{({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma)\in R^{\prime}(\mu)}\bigl(\prod\nolimits_{\hbox to9.39pt{\vbox to14.71pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.14055pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}{}{
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{
}{
}{

{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}q^{a(u)+1}\,t^{l(u)}\bigr){\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}^{-}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r},Y,0^{k},z_{1},\ldots,z_{s},0,\ldots,0;\,t^{-1}),

where
\mu=(\eta;\lambda;\kappa)\in{\mathbb{N}}^{m}
, with
m=r+k+s
, and

R^{\prime}(\mu)
is the same as
R(\mu)
in (
274
), except that

R^{\prime}(\mu)
only contains pairs
({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma)
in which the
augmented ribbons
\nu^{(m-r+1)},\ldots,\nu^{(m)}
of sizes
\eta^{\op}
are non-ragged-right, i.e., no augmentation box in these
ribbons is in a vertical domino.

By Lemma
7.2.5
,
J_{\eta|\lambda|\kappa}(x,Y,z;\,q,t)
has coefficients in
{\mathbb{Z}}[q,t]
.

Proposition 7.3.2
.

The specialized integral form Macdonald polynomials in
(
282
), symmetric in
y_{1},\ldots,y_{n}
,
converge
t
-adically (§
2.6
) to

(287)

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}{\mathcal{E}}_{(\eta;\,0^{n};\,\lambda;\,\kappa)}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r},y_{1},\ldots,y_{n},0^{k},z_{1},\ldots,z_{s};\,q,t)=J_{\eta|\lambda|\kappa}(x,Y,z;\,q,t).

If
\eta\in({\mathbb{Z}}_{>0})^{r}
, then, for each
n
, the factors

(1-q^{a(u)+1}t^{l(u)+1})
for boxes
u
of content
-1
in the top

r
rows of
\dg((\eta;\,0^{n};\,\lambda;\,\kappa))
are given
by
(1-q^{\eta_{i}}t^{l_{i}+n+1})
, where
l_{i}
are the legs
when
n=0
, and the product of these factors divides
{\mathcal{E}}_{(\eta;\,0^{n};\,\lambda;\,\kappa)}(x;q,t)
. In this case, the
specialized integral forms without these factors converge strongly:
specifically, for all
n
, we have

(288)

\frac{{\mathcal{E}}_{(\eta;\,0^{n};\,\lambda;\,\kappa)}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r},y_{1},\ldots,y_{n},0^{k},z_{1},\ldots,z_{s};\,q,t)}{\prod_{i=1}^{r}(1-q^{\eta_{i}}t^{l_{i}+n+1})}=J_{\eta|\lambda|\kappa}[x,\,y_{1}+\cdots+y_{n},z;\,q,t].

Note that when the strong convergence in
(
288
) holds, it implies the
t
-adic
convergence in (
287
), because
{\prod_{i=1}^{r}(1-q^{\eta_{i}}t^{l_{i}+n+1})}\equiv 1\pmod{(t^{n+1})}
. As a special case, when
r=0
and
\eta
is
empty, (
288
) holds and reduces to

(289)

{\mathcal{E}}_{(0^{n};\,\lambda;\,\kappa)}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{n},0^{k},z_{1},\ldots,z_{s};\,q,t)=J_{\emptyset|\lambda|\kappa}[y_{1}+\cdots+y_{n},z;\,q,t].

Proof.

Substituting
(z_{1},\ldots,z_{k+s})\mapsto(0^{k},z_{1},\ldots,z_{s})
in the result for
\eta^{\prime}=\eta
,

\lambda^{\prime}=\emptyset
,
\kappa^{\prime}=(\lambda;\kappa)
gives the
result for
\eta
,
\lambda
,
\kappa
. This reduces the problem
to the case that
\lambda=\emptyset
. We will show that the limits
hold term by term in the expansions (
274
) for
{\mathcal{E}}_{(\eta;\,0^{n};\,\kappa)}(x,y,z;q,t)
and
(
286
) for
J_{\eta\,|\,\emptyset\,|\,\kappa}(x,Y,z;q,t)
.

Let
({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma)\in R
index a term in (
274
) for

\mu=(\eta;\,0^{n};\,\kappa)
. Thus,
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
is a tuple of
augmented ribbons, with flag bounds
b_{i}=m+1-i
in the
augmentation boxes, where
m=r+n+s
. From top to bottom,
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}

consists of
r
ribbons of lengths
\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{r}
, then

n
of length zero, then
s
of lengths
\kappa_{1},\ldots,\kappa_{s}
, with flag numbers
1,\ldots,m
, as sketched below.

(290)

\begin{array}[]{cc}\hbox to166.3pt{\vbox to156.25pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 35.76595pt\lower-6.66634pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

{\boldsymbol{\nu}}&\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}\end{array}

Let
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}
be the tuple obtained by deleting the
n
ribbons of
length zero with flag numbers
r+1,\ldots,r+n
from
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
, and
let
\widehat{\sigma}
be a compatible permutation that assigns flag numbers

r+s,\ldots,2,1
to the augmentation boxes of
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}
. Then,
for flag bounds
\widehat{b}
such that
\widehat{b}_{i}=i
for
i=1,\ldots,r

and
\widehat{b}_{i}=n+i
for
i=r+1,\ldots,r+s
, each augmentation box
in
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}
gets the same flag bound as the corresponding box in

{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
, as in the sketch above on the right. The terms in
(
286
) for
J_{\eta\,|\,\emptyset\,|\,\kappa}(x,Y,z;q,t)
are indexed by the set
R^{\prime}
of pairs

(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}},\widehat{\sigma})
corresponding to
({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma)\in R

for which none of the augmentation boxes with flag number
1,\ldots,r

is in a vertical domino.

Equating variable names

(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r},y_{1},\ldots,y_{n},z_{1},\ldots,z_{s})
with

(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})
, the polynomial
{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}^{-}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m},0,\ldots,0;\,t^{-1})
is the sum of

(-t)^{m(T)}t^{-\inv(T)}x^{|T|}
over non-attacking flagged
tableaux
T\in\FSST({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma,{\mathcal{A}}^{b}_{\pm})
with
entries in
[m]_{\pm}
, i.e., fillings
T\colon{\boldsymbol{\nu}}\rightarrow[m]_{\pm}
such that the extended tableau
T^{\prime}
on
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}_{{\boldsymbol{\circ}}}

with flag bounds
m,\ldots,1
is a non-attacking super tableau.
Identifying the components of
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}
with components of

{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
, let
\widehat{T}
be the restriction of
T
to
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}
.
Since the flag bounds
\widehat{b}
on
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\circ}}}
match the
flag bounds on
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}_{{\boldsymbol{\circ}}}
,
\widehat{T}\in\FSST(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}},\widehat{\sigma},{\mathcal{A}}^{\widehat{b}}_{\pm})
is a non-attacking flagged
tableau with entries in
[m]_{\pm}
.

If
\eta_{i}>0
for any
i\leq r
, and
u
is the box of content
-1

in the component
\nu^{(m+1-i)}
with flag bound
i
, then
u

attacks the augmentation boxes of
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
with flag bound
j
for
all
i<j\leq m
. Hence
|T(u)|\leq i
for all non-attacking tableaux

T
. This implies that
T
has no attacking inversions involving the
augmentation boxes with flag bounds
r+1,\ldots,r+n
, and therefore
that
\inv(\widehat{T})=\inv(T)
.

Corresponding boxes
u\in{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
and
\widehat{u}\in\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}
have
the same arm and leg, except for boxes of content
-1
in the
components with flag bounds
i\leq r
. For these boxes,
l(u)=l(\widehat{u})+n
, due to the contribution from the
n
extra components of
length zero in
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
. For
T
and
\widehat{T}
as above,
Lemma
7.2.5
, gives
n(\mu_{+})+\sum_{\resizebox{327880}{}{\hbox to9.39pt{\vbox to14.71pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.14055pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

.
Hence, if any of the augmentation boxes with flag numbers

is in a vertical domino in
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}_{{\boldsymbol{\circ}}}
, then

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}l(u)-\inv(T)\geq n
. For
such
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
, we therefore have

(291)

t^{n(\mu_{+})}\bigl(\prod\nolimits_{\hbox to9.39pt{\vbox to14.71pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.14055pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

and the contribution to the
t
-adic limit as
n\rightarrow\infty

from these terms vanishes.

If no augmentation box with flag bound
1,\ldots,r
is in a vertical
domino, then every box
u
of content
-1
in a component with flag
bound
i\leq r
forms a horizontal domino with the augmentation box,
forcing
|T(u)|\leq i
with or without the non-attacking condition.
Hence
\inv(T)=\inv(\widehat{T})
for every
T
, and
T\rightarrow\widehat{T}
is a weight-preserving bijection of both non-attacking and
unrestricted tableaux, giving

(292)

{\mathcal{G}}_{{\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma}^{-}(x,y,z,0,\ldots,0;\,t^{-1})={\mathcal{G}}_{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}},\widehat{\sigma}}^{-}[x,Y,z,0,\ldots,0;t^{-1}]\quad\text{with $Y=y_{1}+\cdots+y_{n}$.}

This proves (
287
).

Assume now that
\eta\in({\mathbb{Z}}_{>0})^{r}
. Then every component of

{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
with flag bound
i\leq r
contains a box
u_{i}
of content

-1
, and these boxes attack each other. Since
|T(u_{i})|\leq i
for
every non-attacking
T\in\FSST({\boldsymbol{\nu}},\sigma,{\mathcal{A}}^{b}_{\pm})

with entries in
[m]_{\pm}
it follows that in fact
|T(u_{i})|=i
.
For
a,a^{\prime}\in[m]_{\pm}
, the conditions
a^{\prime}\preccurlyeq a
and
a\prec a^{\prime}
each depend only on
|a|
. Hence, if the domino formed by
u_{i}

and the augmentation box with flag bound
i
is horizontal, we can
change its orientation to vertical and change
T(u_{i})
from
i
to

\overline{i}
, while keeping the entries in all other boxes unchanged
except for the shift in position, as in the example with
i=2
shown
here.

This multiplies the term in
\bigl(\prod\nolimits_{\resizebox{327880}{}{\hbox to9.39pt{\vbox to14.71pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.14055pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

for each non-attacking tableau by

-q^{a(u_{i})+1}t^{l(u_{i})+1}
, where the factor
-t
comes from the
one entry
i
that changes to
\overline{i}
. We have
a(u_{i})+1=\eta_{i}
, and, as noted previously,
l(u_{i})=l_{i}+n
, where

l_{i}=l(\widehat{u_{i}})
is the leg of the corresponding box for

n=0
. Fixing
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
with all boxes
u_{i}
in horizontal
dominoes, it follows that the terms in (
274
) for all

2^{r}
orientations of these dominoes sum to

(294)

\prod_{i=1}^{r}(1-q^{\eta_{i}}t^{l_{i}+n+1})\,\times\,\bigl(\prod\nolimits_{\hbox to9.39pt{\vbox to14.71pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.14055pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

\prod\nolimits_{\resizebox{327880}{}{\hbox to9.39pt{\vbox to14.71pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.14055pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}q^{a(u)+1}\,t^{l(u)}
is for the given
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
with all
r
dominoes
horizontal. Combined with (
292
), this proves
(
